| Composable storefront may use its own login page instead of the authorization server login page under authorization code flow only if `authserver.oauthclientdetails.loginpageuri.allowed.hosts` lists the storefront hosts (each subdomain listed separately); the property is read only at system startup and cannot be changed at runtime. SAP's own setup also sets `cookies.SameSite.enabled=true` | 2211-jdk21 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/cfcf687ce2544bba9799aa6c8314ecd0/928ce101922944e3b546cf39d9831a4d.html | 2026-09-04 |
| Composable storefront front-end floor: Angular >= 21.2.0, Angular CLI >= 21.2.0, Node.js >= 22.22.0, npm >= 10.9.2; the latest back-end patch is required. Library access needs an S-user with the Composable Storefront licence in SAP's Repository Based Shipment Channel (RBSC) | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/cfcf687ce2544bba9799aa6c8314ecd0/5de67850bd8d487181fef9c9ba59a31d.html | 2026-09-04 |

| OCC extension architecture: extension names end in `occ` (e.g. `acceleratorocc`, `b2bocc`), they depend on `commercewebservices` and are imported into its Spring web context, so they need no separate webapp or install command. All controller endpoints across active OCC extensions must have unique paths; `occ.rewrite.overlapping.paths.enabled` is the documented remedy for collisions | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/d46d19516961438f8939718e87ed787b.html | 2026-09-04 |
| `b2bocc` (module bin/modules/b2b-accelerator) exposes the B2B Accelerator functionality as OCC API and is NOT deprecated; the separate `b2boccaddon` (OCC AddOn, same module) carries planned deletion Q1 2027. `acceleratorwebservicesaddon` (module core-accelerator) also carries planned deletion Q1 2027 | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/4b3f9603358e4c4e906fd1f0f0fae684.html | 2026-09-04 |

| There is NO Extension named `sapcentralpricing`. The documented SAP pricing options at IEP 2211 are the SAP Synchronous Pricing module (JCo/BOL), the SAP S/4HANA Order Management Integration module, and SAP Omnichannel Promotion Pricing via the Reuse Services module (sapppspricing). Neither the Synchronous Pricing module nor the SAP Availability module (sapproductavailability, b2bsapproductavailability, sapproductavailabilitybackoffice) ships an `*occ` extension, so they have no standard OCC surface | IEP 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/612ca84ab5194f0c89c6216a937bceb0/3b0e263644704b449f3f729b97f2fce6.html | 2026-09-04 |

| The composable storefront Feature Compatibility Matrix is the authoritative per-feature version gate (library version + required SAP Commerce Cloud version). Selected floors: B2B Commerce Quotes 2211.20/2211.20; B2B PunchOut Integration 2211.41/2211.41; Customer Service (ticketing) 5.2/2211; B2B Account Summaries, B2B Unit-Level Orders, B2B Payment Controls, B2B Organization User Registration, Multi-Site Support and Expert Mode for Configurable Products each 5.1/2211; BOPiS 6.0/2211; Future Stock 6.0/2205; Bulk Pricing 3.2/2105; Saved Cart 3.2/2105; Cart Import and Export 4.2/2105; Quick Order 4.1/2105; Scheduled Replenishment 3.0/2105; Consignment Tracking 1.2/2105; Cancellations and Returns 1.4/2105; Variants 1.5/2105; Inventory Display 4.1/2105; Store Locator 1.2/2105; Multi-Dimensional Products and CAPTCHA 2211.28; OPPS Coupon Code Integration 2211.24/2211.24, OPPS Login Required 2211.24/2211.24 and OPPS Promotions on Product Details Page 2211.20/2211.20; Site Theming and Site Theme Switcher 2211.29; WalkMe Integration 2211.29; Keyword Redirects 2211.24; Recent Searches 2211.20; Trending Search Terms 2211.31 | composable storefront 2211 / 221121.x | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/6c7b98dbe68f4a508cac17a207182f4c/7f4265e58242466aba453632401cdff4.html | 2026-09-04 |
| Composable storefront features that require the SAP S/4HANA Order Management Integration (back end + front end) and their floors: Estimated Delivery Schedules 5.1/2211; Requested Delivery Date 6.4/2211.9 (library `@spartacus/requested-delivery-date`, integration extension pack 2211.9+, plus a Backoffice switch); Invoice Display 6.5/2211.12; SAP Order Management for Sourcing and Availability 2211.24/2211.24; SAP S/4HANA Service Integration 2211.28/2211.28. SAP Enterprise Product Development Visualization is marked DEPRECATED in the same matrix | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/962112809f9a48f9b36aa05b208b3731/22a716d07988401c8c25d8d1f4c2df43.html | 2026-09-04 |
| The composable storefront libraries installable via schematics are `@spartacus/` asm, cart (Saved Cart + Quick Order + Cart Import/Export), cdc, cds, checkout (basic + b2b + b2b scheduled replenishment), cpq-quote, order, organization, pickup-in-store, product (Bulk Pricing + Variants + Image Zoom), product-configurator, qualtrics, quote, smartedit, storefinder, tracking (Tag Management + Personalization), user - plus core and schematics. B2C and B2B storefronts cannot run together in one storefront application | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/cfcf687ce2544bba9799aa6c8314ecd0/e38d45609de04412920a7fc9c13d41e3.html | 2026-09-04 |
| Composable storefront order approval is part of the Organization library: purchase limits (permissions) put an order on hold after Place Order, the order status goes Created then Pending with an approval table naming the reason and the approver, and the approver approves or rejects with an optional comment from the Approval Dashboard under My Account | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/d1ca9a1517f847a5b57b4e08400807c1/f39b845d3c14460fa2cfd449f6c57503.html | 2026-09-04 |
| Composable storefront customer ticketing is `@spartacus/customer-ticketing` ("Customer Service"), requires SAP Commerce Cloud 2211+ and the `customerticketingocc` Extension; users create and view requests, add messages and attachments (exactly one attachment per message) and change status; the agent side stays in the Backoffice Customer Support Cockpit | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/7ecc65667bf34f19a2d4917fc18642f7.html | 2026-09-04 |
| Composable storefront authentication is OAuth 2.1 with authorization code + PKCE as the default grant. Required feature toggles in spartacus-features.module.ts: authorizationCodeFlowByDefault, incrementProcessesCountForMergeCart, dispatchLoginActionOnlyWhenTokenReceived, cdsLoginEventsToken, asyncAuthConfigInitializer (221121.15+). They are on by default from 221121.1; storefronts updated from 2211.43 or older must enable them. Implementation is based on the angular-oauth2-oidc library | composable storefront 221121.x | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/7e237cc1245c43219e0a957a2cfa81c2.html | 2026-09-04 |
| The complete documented set of composable storefront integrations is: B2B PunchOut, Configurable Products (SAP Variant Configuration and Pricing), SAP CPQ Configurable Products, SAP Customer Data Cloud, SAP Digital Payments, SAP EPD Visualization, Intelligent Selling Services, Invoice Display, Kyma, My Account SAP Integration, Omnichannel Promotion and Pricing, SAP Subscription Billing, SAP Open Payment Framework, SAP S/4HANA Order Management, Requested Delivery Date, SAP S/4HANA Service, SAP Personalization Connectivity, Qualtrics. There is NO integration for a generic external OIDC identity provider or Microsoft Entra ID - SAP Customer Data Cloud (`@spartacus/cdc`) is the only standard identity integration | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/962112809f9a48f9b36aa05b208b3731/1c59d38bd56941aeb15c8333dfa0d780.html | 2026-09-04 |
| Configurable bundles and configurable guided selling have NO composable storefront feature: "Configurable Bundle Functionality" is documented for the 2211 back end, "Configurable Guided Selling" only up to 1905, and neither appears in the composable Feature Compatibility Matrix. | Composable storefront i18n is i18next plus i18next-http-backend, and SAP explicitly does NOT support custom usage of i18next in the application ("composable storefront does not support custom usage of i18next"). External translation resources MUST be served over HTTPS (documented man-in-the-middle risk). The JDK 17 August 2025 update got its validity extended from six to twelve months with security fixes until the end of Q2 2026 | 2211-jdk21 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/8f27d88c881f44988000788362909617.html | 2026-09-04 |

| CCv2 mechanism for secrets that must stay out of the code repository: a ZIP static file set of *.properties files uploaded in the Cloud Portal under the name `<environment name>_properties_hcs_platform_<aspect>`, where aspect is accstorefront, backoffice, backgroundProcessing or `common` for all aspects. The secrets are injected into the platform instance on the next deployment to that environment. Such a file must never be attached to an environment endpoint | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/0fa6bcf4736c46f78c248512391eb467/557da644ed1949de9d40a3e4615d8b9b.html | 2026-09-04 |
| SAP Commerce ships a credentials framework for external-system authentication, maintained in Backoffice Administration Cockpit under System / API / Credentials and assigned to consumed destinations (outbound) or exposed destinations (inbound). Credential types: Basic Credential (user name and password), Consumed Certificate Credential, Consumed OAuth Credential (client id, OAuth URL, secret), Exposed OAuth Credential (references an OAuth client). This, not a property file, is the standard home for an outbound third-party credential | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/c36e52141104442fbff92f8d954ff55f.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/84e9127827484aa58551b422e4f99686.html | 2026-09-04 |
| CCv2 network self-service consists of IP Filter Sets (allow/deny lists of source IPs applied to environment endpoints, i.e. INBOUND only), VPN and NAT (up to 10 site-to-site connections to private networks; inbound from VPN restricted to 443, outbound all ports except 22) and Private Link (Private Link Endpoint = outgoing to a customer Azure network). Public-internet traffic permits HTTP and HTTPS, with port 80 redirected to 443 and port 80 disabled by default. There is NO customer-configurable outbound allow-list for public third-party hosts - outbound calls to a public endpoint need no Commerce-side registration | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/ddc1ef9fc7fa48cb90f2b57c061a4012/461c59e18add4521bec9ea4c7b4d2fbf.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/0fa6bcf4736c46f78c248512391eb467/910006b8cf9c4c6fa482984b05a59643.html | 2026-09-04 |
| `commercewebservices` is stateless, but within the scope of a single request a session is still created and used: several filters and interceptors handle cache control, base site and customer verification and set up the request context and session attributes. A service that reads the current base store or current language from session state therefore works unchanged behind OCC, provided the request carries the baseSiteId path segment and the language parameter | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/8b422f4b86691014b579bb20d6432cde.html | 2026-09-04 |
| Composable storefront pages come from the CMS: a new page created in the CMS is picked up automatically and its URL is the CMS label, while a new component must first exist as a CMS component type and is then mapped to an Angular component with ConfigModule.withConfig({cmsComponents: {YOUR_COMPONENT_TYPE: {component: YourComponent}}}). The alternative is a static page plus a static route. So any custom storefront feature needs CMS content types plus an Angular module - it is never OCC-only | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/0bae2d8109d945d5a03743ed3f53ea7a.html | 2026-09-04 |
| SAP ships two properties to detect leftover Log4j 1.x usage: log4j.deprecated.properties.warn (default true) prints a warning when the instance still uses Log4j 1.x configuration properties, and log4j.deprecated.properties.print (default false) lists them. They cover Log4j 1.x CONFIGURATION; Log4j 1.x API calls in custom code keep working only through the log4j-1.2-api bridge | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/b4b7ed2ba3ce40ef8f9d93e95eefba42.html | 2026-09-04 |
| CCv2 communication security: public-internet traffic allows HTTP and HTTPS with port 80 redirected to 443 (port 80 disabled by default); VPN and NAT give up to 10 site-to-site connections to a private network at 650 Mb/s total, inbound from VPN endpoints restricted to 443, outbound from Commerce to VPN endpoints open on ALL ports except 22 (23 and 514 blocked both ways); Private Link Endpoint is the outgoing route to a customer Azure network. SAP states explicitly that with these features in place there is no need for Secure Network Communications (SNC). So an outbound RFC route to an on-premise ERP is a documented CCv2 capability via VPN/NAT or Private Link, and there is no outbound allow list for public hosts | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/ddc1ef9fc7fa48cb90f2b57c061a4012/461c59e18add4521bec9ea4c7b4d2fbf.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Type codes: the `typecode` of a `<deployment>` must be a positive integer between 0 and 32767 and unique platform-wide because it is part of PK generation. 0-10000 are reserved for SAP internal use; values above 10000 are "generally free" but with many documented exceptions (b2bcommerce 100xx, commons 132xx, xprint 244xx/245xx, processing 327xx), the full list being `platform/ext/core/resources/core/unittest/reservedTypecodes.txt`. A duplicate fails the build with "duplicate deployment code". Deployment strings are limited to 24 characters, and SAP recommends a deployment only for direct subtypes of GenericItem | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8c6254f086691014b095a08a61d1efed.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The inbound PATCH API of the Integration API is a partial update: attributes omitted from the payload keep their values, but a collection provided with fewer values DELETES the existing values and persists only those sent. It needs the integration key in the URL, updates one item per request, supports no query filters, requires membership of integrationadmingroup or integrationcreategroup, and is recorded by Inbound Monitoring. This is the standard route to merge-style inbound updates without changing platform collection semantics | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/25aa5bc96b3e4ceb958a4aee039db7c1.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Using the Outbound Service Facade requires three inputs - the platform item, a ConsumedDestination and an IntegrationObject describing the payload - placed into a `SyncParameters` instance passed to one of the facade's `send(...)` methods. Resolving the integration object and the destination before the call is therefore the documented pattern, not an anti-pattern; `outboundservices` alone gives access to the facade, `integrationbackoffice` adds outbound request monitoring. Outbound options are Outbound Sync (scheduled), Webhooks (near real time) and the Outbound Service (custom code, synchronous processing) | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/d3c8f9e63854422e99751de57dbceef5.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The 2211 Scripting Engine is JSR-223 based and supports three languages out of the box: Groovy, BeanShell and JavaScript (more can be added from the JSR-223 list). Because Nashorn was removed from the JDK at 15, a GraalVM/Truffle JavaScript engine is on the platform classpath by design on the 2211-jdk21 line - relevant when judging custom code that imports `com.oracle.truffle.js.*` | 2211 / 2211-jdk21 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8bec04a386691014938a9996a977d07f.html | 2026-09-05 |
| OCC caching is standard in two layers. Client side: the `@CacheControl` annotation from `webservicescommons` emits the Cache-Control header, applies to GET and HEAD only and needs `CacheControlHandlerInterceptor` in the mvc interceptors (ProductsController uses PRIVATE / maxAge 120). Server side: Spring `<cache:annotation-driven>` in commercewebservices/web/webroot/WEB-INF/config/common/cache-config-spring.xml, cache manager `wsCacheManager` = EhCacheCacheManager over `TenantAwareEhCacheManagerFactoryBean` (ehcache.xml, named caches fieldSetCache, productSearchCache, ...), methods annotated `@Cacheable`. The default key generator `commerceCacheKeyGenerator` (CommerceCacheKeyGenerator) ALWAYS adds the base site identifier and the language ISO code to the key; user id and currency are opt-in via generateKey(true,true,...) | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/8b711228866910149500b73575cb386e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Whether an OCC endpoint is reachable without a login is governed by `BaseSite.requiresAuthentication` (declared in commerceservices-items.xml, default false, switchable in Backoffice under WCMS / Website / Administration). When true, only authenticated users in `customergroup` can call OCC endpoints; every other caller, authenticated or not, is denied. `SecurePortalAuthenticationInterceptor` in `commercewebservicescommons` answers unauthenticated calls to secured endpoints with a 404, and the annotation `@SecurePortalUnauthenticatedAccess` is the documented opt-out that keeps an endpoint public - it must be added to custom endpoints that are meant to stay anonymous. The standard public list is cms components / pages / sitepages, cardtypes, currencies, languages, titles, countries and regions, forgottenpasswordtokens, resetpassword and consenttemplates | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/243154d31d6145a3a2d6e784ac8e380f.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Adding a field to an OCC payload is an Orika-based DTO mapping change, not an item-type change: `DataMapper` plus `FieldSetBuilder` and `GeneralFieldFilter` are configured in commercewebservices/web/webroot/WEB-INF/config/v2/dto-mappings-v2-spring.xml, custom mappers/converters/filters must carry the `WsDTOMapping` annotation (only annotated beans are registered in the Orika mapper factory) or extend `AbstractCustomMapper`, and a single renamed field can be handled by the `FieldMapper` / `fieldMapper` bean. Which fields a response carries per field set (BASIC / DEFAULT / FULL) comes from the fields configuration, so a new attribute must be declared there as well as on the WsDTO | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/8c404c5886691014a48c88f4a49f9bf3.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The standard home for an outbound REST call is the `outboundservices` extension against a ConsumedDestination: `outboundHttpClientFactory` (DefaultOutboundHttpClientFactory) builds the HttpClient on a PoolingHttpClientConnectionManager configured by outboundservices.httpclient.max.connections, .connections.keep-alive, .connections.connectionTimeout and .connections.validity, and the RestTemplate is cached per ConsumedDestination (outboundservices.cache.resttemplate.maxEntries default 1000, eviction LFU, ttlSeconds). So connection pooling, timeouts and RestTemplate reuse are configuration on the standard path rather than custom beans | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/69164fb300aa42f89ba1db7ac1fd297d.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/72c46155371d42c7930344ff544ddd82.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Extension library management: with usemaven="true" the extension's `external-dependencies.xml` is a Maven POM whose declared dependencies the ant build downloads into <extension>/lib, and SAP states that libraries in <extension>/lib "will be added to the global class path of Platform (which is why we need to align with other extensions shipping the same)". Transitive dependencies are NOT downloaded, so every dependency must be declared explicitly. The only scoping alternative is a second external-dependencies.xml under web/webroot/WEB-INF, whose jars load into the web application class loader only - there is no build-only scope for the root file. SAP's own guidance: "As soon as you find yourself committing a JAR file to your extension, you're actually doing it wrong" | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/120f6d7b89a745018cb28b5e34318fa4.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Composable storefront i18n chunk and namespace mapping is build-time configuration: translation resources are `{lang: {chunkName: {key: value or nested object}}}`, every key belongs to a namespace and every namespace is encapsulated in a chunk, and `i18n.chunks` declares the mapping (e.g. `common: ['searchBox','sorting']`, `cart: ['cartDetails','cartItems']`); no explicit entry is needed only when namespace and chunk name are identical. Keys are camelCase and nested (`cart.cartDetails.proceedToCheckout`, `searchBox.searchHere`, `formErrors.minOneDigit`), NOT lowercase. SAP advises against adding custom keys to existing composable chunks to keep the upgrade path. Missing key in development mode renders `[chunk:key]` (documented example `[common:form.confirm]`), in production a non-breaking space | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/775e61ed219c4999852d43be5244e94a.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The SSR advantage of `i18n.backend.loader` is specific to LOCAL assets: the documented section is "Lazy Loading From Local Project Assets", the loader example is an ES dynamic `import()` of `public/i18n-assets/{lng}/{ns}.json`, and SAP's stated reason is that Webpack loads the wrapped JSON build artifacts directly from the local file system. SAP discourages a local `loadPath` for exactly the opposite reason - it "causes translations to be loaded through HTTP requests, which results in unnecessary HTTP round trips from SSR to the ExpressJS server itself" - and the remote case has its own section which prescribes `loadPath` ("should only be used when storing translations in a remote endpoint"). Consequence: a remote translations endpoint costs the SSR process an HTTP round trip whichever of the two properties is configured; only a dynamic import of local build artifacts avoids it | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/775e61ed219c4999852d43be5244e94a.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Composable storefront site theming is styling only, not a content or translation partition: before 2211.29 the theme was `config.context.theme` (static or populated from the CMS via automatic site-context configuration), from 2211.29 a list of optional themes comes from `config.siteTheme.optionalThemes` plus a theme-switcher dropdown whose choice persists in local storage, and the shipped high-contrast light/dark themes work by overriding global CSS variables such as `--cx-color-background` with SCSS mixins `cx-highContrastTheme*`. There is no composable counterpart to the platform SiteTheme as a data partition key | composable storefront 2211.29+ | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/f7e5208c423d45709e0ef27bdc47fae3.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Predefined composable translation assets must be served by the application itself: SAP's instruction is "You need to serve these files, either from your own custom endpoint, or by copying them into the /assets folder of your Angular application" (`cp ./node_modules/@spartacus/assets/i18n-assets ./public -r`). A custom endpoint serving translation chunks is therefore a documented deployment shape, not a workaround | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/775e61ed219c4999852d43be5244e94a.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The composable storefront has a standard closed-portal feature called **Early Login**: `ConfigModule.withConfig({ routing: { protected: true } })` requires a login for every CMS-driven route (any route carrying `CmsPageGuard`); the routes `login`, `register`, `forgot password` and `reset password` are public by default in `default-routing-config.ts`, and any other route is opened again with `protected: false` in its own route config. SAP documents it explicitly for password-protected B2B stores. Individual-route protection should use guarded CMS components instead | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/c190d4cdb1ae4e698f413ec9470fcc17.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `BaseSite.requiresAuthentication` is documented as the OCC-side half of the Early Login and Registration features (page title "Securing OCC APIs", Accelerators 2211); the property was MOVED to `BaseSite` from its original location, so a `CMSSite.requiresAuthentication` reference is the pre-move name | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/243154d31d6145a3a2d6e784ac8e380f.html | 2026-09-05 |
| User-account brute-force login protection is platform core, independent of the accelerator storefront: the maximum number of unsuccessful login attempts is set per user group as `Max brute force login attempts` (Backoffice User / User Groups / Administration; the minimum value wins across several groups, and it never applies to `admin`, for which `admin.login.enabled=false` is the documented control). Failed attempts are persisted in `BruteForceLoginAttempts` items (cluster-safe, survive restarts), exceeding the value sets `User.loginDisabled=true` and creates a `BruteForceLoginDisabledAudit`; access tokens of disabled users are deleted and further token requests fail with HTTP 400 `invalid_grant` / "User is disabled". The extension point is `de.hybris.platform.servicelayer.user.UserAuditLoginStrategy` registered as bean `userAuditLoginStrategy`; the default logs via `DefaultUserAuditLoginStrategy` | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/28cfbf93784640d09929ee4e9b97ba1e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Locked user accounts and OAuth clients unlock automatically: `platform.authentication.password.bruteforce.unlock.wait.time.seconds` (default 300) and `platform.authentication.password.bruteforce.unlock.factor` (default 3, multiplying the lock duration on each repeat lock). The unlock timestamp is visible in Backoffice as "OAuth user login disabled until specific date/time" under System / OAuth / OAuth Clients and under User / Employee and User / Customer. This page is part of the JDK 21 authorization-server documentation set | 2211-jdk21 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/4be02125ff2f447eb9b172368a0fece8.html | 2026-09-05 |
| OAuth CLIENT brute-force prevention is separate from user brute-force prevention: `oauth2.maxAuthenticationAttempts` (not enabled by default) disables the client after that many failed client authentications, subsequent attempts answer HTTP 401 "Authentication for clientId:'x' is disabled", public clients (no client secret in the database) are deliberately excluded, and re-enabling happens in Backoffice under OAuth / Clients or through automatic unlocking. In flows needing both client and user credentials, a failed client authentication counts only for the client | 2211-jdk21 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/727a7b08e93e489fbe767a9797e80d89.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Failed user login attempts are stored in `BruteForceLoginAttempts` and failed OAuth-client attempts in the newer `SAPBruteForceOAuthLoginAttempts` model; the split requires a deployment in migration data mode | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/4b0d70fbac754e02b5c712037467bfe6.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Return-to-the-requested-page after login is standard in the composable storefront auth layer: `AuthGuard` protects pages for logged-in users and `NotAuthGuard` pages for anonymous users, both call `AuthRedirectService` with the accessed page, and "when you log in, you are redirected to the page you were on (or tried to access) before logging in". The redirect URL is held by `AuthRedirectStorageService` and synchronised to `localStorage` by `AuthStatePersistenceService` via `StatePersistenceService`. Under the redirecting flows the storefront saves the previous URL before sending the user to the authorization-server login page (`LoginGuard` / `LoginRouteModule`; `CustomLoginGuard` for the custom login page) and returns to it afterwards. SAP recommends using both guards consistently on all pages precisely to keep the redirects correct | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/dc307a52881548e9a15aa6fcba6f7587.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `secureportaladdon` WAS a real SAP Extension: the "B2B Early Login AddOn", documented up to 2105 (early login plus B2B account request approved by `b2bregistrationapprovergroup`, item type `CMSSecurePortalRestriction`, the B2B registration e-mail processes). It was deprecated and DELETED with release 2205; SAP's upgrade guide instructs moving all B2B customer registration code to `b2bcommerce` and `b2bcommercefacades` | 2105 (deleted 2205) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/8aeddd34866910149de2d448ff18df98.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bbcd2978537e4ae298479557c0b8fdbd/69c0d5d94a5942b3a56326003b323613.html | 2026-09-05 |

Documented cleanup: HAC / Maintenance / Cleanup, button "Clean all orphaned types". | `yacceleratorcockpits` is SAP's own TEMPLATE extension whose documented purpose is (a) to hold all cockpit Spring bean redefinitions in one place - the global file `yacceleratorcockpits-spring.xml` registered via `yacceleratorcockpits.application-context` and therefore effective for the WHOLE platform context (SAP's own example globally aliases `calculationService`), (b) per-cockpit web contexts injected via `<cockpit>.additionalWebSpringConfigs` (SAP's example redefines bean `ProductPerspective` in `productcockpit-web.xml`), and (c) redefinition of cockpit UI configuration imported into the database. Deprecated with 6.6 (module base-accelerator). | Cockpit deletion page, path forward per extension: cockpit / admincockpit / productcockpit / configurablebundlecockpits / subscriptioncockpits / subscriptionbundlecockpits -> Backoffice; cmscockpit -> SmartEdit (with a separate "SmartEdit Feature Parity" page); yacceleratorcockpits -> `yacceleratorbackoffice`. SAP states Backoffice and SmartEdit "already expose MOST of the features" of the cockpits and that parity will be increased "in the future" - so feature parity is explicitly NOT complete. | The 2211 Deprecation Status table contains NO row for `cscockpit` or `reportcockpit`, and neither does the 2005 table (which still lists cockpit, admincockpit, importcockpit, productcockpit, yacceleratorcockpits, configurablebundlecockpits, subscriptioncockpits, subscriptionbundlecockpits and cmscockpit, all with announced deprecation 6.6). Both extensions were therefore de-released before 2005 and are correctly NOT on the framework-update deletion list | 2005 / 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/12be4ac419604b01aabb1adeb2c4c8a2/8bb15ed586691014a948d1553f4947cf.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `yacceleratorfulfilmentprocess` is a TEMPLATE extension "designed to be used with modulegen and extgen to create the fulfillment and order management processes required for projects"; SAP instructs "Begin by modifying the generated extension and not the template". By default it ships only two processes, order-process and consignment-process, described as "skeleton processes representing the high level steps that most fulfillment processes require. It is expected these will be changed before use". The process a store uses is chosen by configuration on the BaseStore, so one process can serve all stores or a tailored process per store. Order splitting comes from basecommerce and is extended by adding new OrderSplittingStrategies | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/8afaf56686691014aa51dda271a43ee5.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Order splitting: `orderSplittingService` = `de.hybris.platform.ordersplitting.impl.DefaultOrderSplittingService` with a `strategiesList`; SAP's documented default list has `splitByAvailableCount` COMMENTED OUT, leaving splitByDeliveryMode, splitByNamedDeliveryDate and splitByWarehouse. A custom strategy implements `SplittingStrategy` (perform + afterSplitting) or extends `AbstractSplittingStrategy`; declaring the service with no strategy yields exactly one consignment. Split by warehouse is documented as an optimisation that matches as many entries as possible to one warehouse, and "If more than one warehouse can fulfill the order, one from the set is randomly chosen" - `ConsignmentService.getWarehouse` "simply randomly returns any one of the warehouses that can realize delivery". So RANDOM warehouse choice is SAP's designed behaviour, not a defect | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/94d0e80f20c84bdca999b1b8ee3f2b14.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Warehouse Integration is explicitly "a simple reference interface. It is meant to provide an example or foundation on which you can build your own interface". `Process2WarehouseAdapter` declares prepareConsignment and shipConsignment (platform to warehouse); `Warehouse2ProcessAdapter.receiveConsignmentStatus(ConsignmentModel, WarehouseConsignmentStatus)` is the inbound side. `WarehouseConsignmentStatus` has exactly three values: CANCEL, PARTIAL, COMPLETE. SAP ships a mock implementation that on prepareConsignment "immediately calls receiveConsignmentStatus with status COMPLETE" and whose shipConsignment is empty | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/542b4514a5684474881d9099968015d0.html | 2026-09-05 |
| OCC cancellation and return calls at 2211: `POST /occ/v2/{baseSiteId}/users/{userId}/orders/{code}/cancellation` (body cancellationRequestEntryInputs) and `POST /occ/v2/{baseSiteId}/users/{userId}/orderReturns` (body orderCode plus returnRequestEntryInputs). The order detail response carries a `cancellable` flag and a `returnable` flag; the documented prerequisite for a return is "A user who has an order that is partially shipped", so the return path is gated by order status, not only by the process code | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/48d55e5c0c834b5d83876d57ab5a5a3a.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Composable storefront Cancellations and Returns is documented as a **B2C** storefront feature, requires back end 2005.0 or newer, and SAP advises also installing Order Management in the back end. It is DISABLED by default and "Since the feature is CMS-driven, the only way to enable it is through the CMS": make `AccountOrderDetailsActionsComponent` visible and replace `AccountOrderHistoryComponent` with `AccountOrderHistoryTabContainer` in the `BodyContent-orders` slot. "No special configuration is required" and "No special extensibility is available for this feature" | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/25194600d51a4bbba5c5c2dd15d7bcfe.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The standard return path with an SAP back end is the SAP Return Order module: ysaperpreturnprocess, sapreturnsbackoffice, sapreturnsexchange, sapreturnsexchangeb2b, ysapomsreturnprocess, sapcpireturnsexchange, sapcpiomsreturnsexchange and `returnsupport` (one s, not returnssupport). `returnsupport` is a template extension that "listens to the event CreateReturnEvent through the listener CreateReturnEventListener and then triggers the return process associated with the basestore", and overrides createReturnRequest / createRefund plus DefaultReturnActionAdapter. Configuration is the BaseStore field "Return Request Process code" in Backoffice under Base Commerce / Base Store / Administration: `sap-oms-return-process` with Order Management Services, `sap-return-process` with Cart and Checkout | IEP 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/acdf6ddda9fe4e4cbd420baf8db4396b/c2c0e4f133864676bbe37a86f3486a8e.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/acdf6ddda9fe4e4cbd420baf8db4396b/5b1d5ec46f34446cb826da32fd3412c9.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Returns Service: `WarehousingReturnService` lives in the `warehousing` extension and extends `DefaultReturnService` of basecommerce, adding validation (no item returned twice) and finalizeReturnRequest, which by default sets ReturnStatus APPROVAL_PENDING (for BORIS: marks the request Received and creates an RMA). Crucially: "Once a return request is created, the return service does not need to be used. Instead, a return process is started at request creation. This process should be used to further modify the state of the return." So ALL post-creation return state changes live in the return process | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/8c4522d386691014bd79f78d6434ca81.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `ysaporderfulfillment` is likewise an extgen/modulegen TEMPLATE extension ("Begin by modifying the generated extension and not the template") for asynchronous order management with SAP S/4HANA or SAP ERP as the fulfilment system. At checkout submit "a business process instance of the sap-order-process in the SAP Commerce Cloud is created"; its central action is sendOrderToErp and status updates arrive as business process events raised by a custom ImpEx translator, with more advanced statuses never overwritten by less advanced ones. The process is "tailored for complete deliveries only", order cancellation is NOT modelled in it and stale fulfilment processes are terminated by a dedicated cron job. Instances are monitored in Backoffice under System / Business Process, searchable by order code | IEP 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/8ce6157b995e418093b6e5410bcd74b2/8c94cd8086691014ad00da13fd3740e8.html | 2026-09-05 |
| CCv2 cron job node placement: "Unless triggered manually from Backoffice, any cron jobs that aren't assigned to any node groups are run on the backgroundProcessing aspect." To pin a job to an aspect, set `nodeGroup=<name>` on the CronJob AND add that name to the aspect's `cluster.node.groups` in manifest.json. A cron job assigned to a node group can be executed by any node in the group and is taken over by another node if the executing node stops; `nodeID` overrules `nodeGroup` | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/56f44b5119754a7ab2bca7c01a394221.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8b55b41a86691014a150c099a4131a50.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The `processing` extension provides the process engine, the task service and the cronjob service together. The task service "enables you to schedule time-based or event-based actions, even in a cluster environment" and is the lighter alternative to a cron job. `de.hybris.platform.core.TenantAwareThreadFactory` is the documented way to give a self-created thread access to the tenant session - its threads set a tenant and activate a JaloSession before the runnable and deactivate afterwards; Spring's `ThreadPoolTaskScheduler` uses a thread factory that is NOT tenant aware unless TenantAwareThreadFactory is injected as a bean. So a bare `new Thread(...)` with a hand-set tenant has two documented standard replacements | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/cd717a7657b6475fab602d02c16e252b.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/6a782448755941d5adb22b8e39664a2b.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The Data Retention Framework is the standard mechanism for pruning instances of any type: configurable retention rules (which instances, retention period, which cleanup logic), data providers implementing `RetentionItemsProvider` that "return batch items in an iteration", the cron job `AfterRetentionCleanupJobPerformable` that executes the retention logic, and `RetentionCleanupAction` for custom logic (basicRemoveCleanupAction for simple removal). This, not a hand-written FlexibleSearch with a date bound and paging, is the supported way to bound growing tables such as BusinessProcess and ProcessTask | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/2268d55f40c94c5e8c43fa47f3b609a4.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Fraud detection: `FraudService` fronts all `FraudServiceProvider` implementations. SAP ships a standard symptom-scoring provider needing no external system (the documented API example calls it with provider name 'Hybris') but states it "should be used in combination with a third-party service provider as a first-level check in order to reduce the number of paid requests to the third party provider". The result is "a numerical score, not a positive or negative decision regarding whether an order is fraudulent" - the caller interprets it - and `FraudReport` plus `FraudSymptomScoring` are the item types for persisting it | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/b4e9e3c0f3384c1ca0ec1b9b31c5edc6.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Event listener registration: the documented pattern is a Spring bean with `parent="abstractEventListener"`, and "The event system scans the application context and automatically registers all event listeners". The same page also shows a listener bean declared WITHOUT the parent attribute, and `eventService.registerEventListener(...)` is the runtime alternative. Consequence for code review: a missing `parent="abstractEventListener"` is a deviation from the documented pattern but is not by itself proof that a listener is unregistered | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/07e3db4b90b24eb39522cb26ceb34544.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `acceleratorservices` provides data import and export, e-mail and location services, and ships a documented **payment gateway mock** that integrates with the Accelerator storefront checkout for both Silent Order Post and Hosted Order Page, offered as developer options in the multi-step checkout. The extension page carries no deprecation notice at 2211, so depending on it is safe from a de-release view | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/8abddeed86691014be559318fab13d44.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `MockitoAnnotations.initMocks(Object)` is marked `@Deprecated` in the Mockito source with the documented replacement `MockitoAnnotations#openMocks(Object)`; the Javadoc states no version. openMocks returns a closeable resource that should stay open for the duration of the test, whereas initMocks closes it immediately, which breaks static mocks and custom MockMakers. Relevant because mockito-core 5.17.0 ships on the 2211-jdk21 platform, so initMocks still compiles | mockito-core 5.x | https://github.com/mockito/mockito/blob/main/mockito-core/src/main/java/org/mockito/MockitoAnnotations.java | 2026-09-05 |
| Generic audit is ON by default: `auditing.enabled` default **true** ("Auditing is enabled globally by default through the auditing.enabled=true property. To disable it, set auditing.enabled=false in your environment configuration"), `auditing.alltypes.enabled` default true, `auditing.blacklist` default itemsynctimestamp,joblog,logfile,jobmedia,task,taskcondition,processtasklog,businessprocess,cronjob,CronJobHistory, and `audit.<typeCode>.enabled` is true out of the box for about 40 types incl. user, address, cart, cartentry, order, orderentry, product, quote, quoteentry, usergroup, b2bunit, sapb2bdocument, consent, consenttemplate, csticket, employee, basesite, saporder. Further keys: audit.<typeCode>.blacklistedProperties, audit.blacklistedProperties.obfuscationValue (default ****), audit.conditional.session-cart.enabled (true), audit.conditional.fullSpELSupport (false), audit.write.jdbc.batch.size (50). `audit.enabled` is NOT a platform key | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/98830906a27446a5b4c5617be2f88175.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/3e2b8491449d43b391eb1ce9c73cbb7f.html | 2026-09-05 |
| SAP's CCv2 build guidance RECOMMENDS switching generic audit off: under "Performance-Related Properties" the Code Repository Setup page tells customers to add `auditing.enabled=false` (and `default.session.timeout=360`) to the manifest.json `properties` block "to avoid bloating the SAP Commerce Cloud environment database, which can negatively impact memory and response times". manifest.json properties are therefore a documented place to set auditing.enabled, next to Cloud Portal service properties; properties carrying a `persona` attribute are applied only once the environment type is known and are not available at build time | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/d6794b766aea4783b829988dc587f978.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The platform logs the effective audit configuration at startup: "Global auditing enabled: true, auditing for all types enabled: false, explicitly enabled types: [...], blacklisted types: [...]". If global auditing is on, startup additionally verifies that an audit table exists for every auditable type and logs "Invalid audit configuration. The following types are configured for auditing but their audit tables do not exist: ...". Extending the set of audited types requires a system update (on CCv2 a deployment with the Migrate Data option) to generate the tables | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/3e2b8491449d43b391eb1ce9c73cbb7f.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Audit storage is JDBC-based, one table per audited type, named `<deploymentName><numericTypeCode>sn` (SAP's example: the User audit table is `users4sn`). Columns ID, ITEMPK, ITEMTYPEPK, timestamp, currenttimestamp, changinguser, context, payloadbefore, payloadafter, operationtype (0 deletion / 1 creation / 2 modification); many-to-many relation types add sourcePK, targetPK, languagePK. When auditing is enabled globally the audit tables are created up front for every type whether or not it is configured for auditing, and stay empty if it is not. SAP warns "Auditing consumes database space ... it may affect database performance over time" | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/08a27931a21441b59094c8a6aa2a880e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The published `WriteAuditGateway` interface (Spring bean id `writeAuditGateway`) has exactly four methods: saveLinkAuditRecords(List<LinkAuditRecordCommand>), saveTypeAuditRecords(List<TypeAuditRecordCommand>), int removeAuditRecordsForType(String type) and int removeAuditRecordsForType(String type, PK pk). The argument is an ITEM TYPE, SAP's own example being removeAuditRecordsForType("User", user.getPk()) - there is no date-bounded or cut-off variant, so a time-based prune of audit rows is custom code. A further safeguard: "If a particular item is marked as locked against further modifications, it is not possible to remove its audit log entries" | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/83dfce2c78a44589b4377a9bb312777e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The Data Retention Framework DOES reach audit data, but only item-driven: `basicRemoveCleanupAction` (BasicRemoveCleanupAction) "also removes item-related audit records generated by the Generic Audit", and the framework page notes that a retention job using it "only removes audited data if the generic audit feature is enabled". It can therefore prune audit rows together with the audited item, but has no rule shape that prunes audit history of items that still exist. RetentionJobModel carries a required `batchSize` determining how many items a data provider returns per package; each package runs in its own transaction scope and an exception rolls that package back - the standard bounded, resumable batching model | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/2268d55f40c94c5e8c43fa47f3b609a4.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/85b6db0a436a4f8ca344f8009b779f34.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Audit rows feed the GDPR personal data report: generic audit "collects data that you can provide to your customers through personal data reports", audit data is included per type by checking Audit in the Create New Audit Report Data wizard in Backoffice or programmatically via TypeAuditReportConfig.builder().withFullReport() passed to auditViewService.getViewOn(config). Pruning an audit table therefore caps the change history a subject-access request can return for that type | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/89612e1c040c46ff9a6bc2e779f7a10c.html | 2026-09-05 |

| Outbound requests of the Integration API outbound framework are capped at 5 seconds of total execution time (connection plus remote response); on expiry the request is aborted and Outbound Monitoring in the Backoffice Integration UI Tool shows "Request timed out after 5000 ms". A webhook is NOT retried after a timeout; outbound sync retries `outboundsync.max.retries` times. The outbound RESPONSE BODY is limited to 1024 bytes - a larger body is not read into memory and OutboundRequest.error reads "The error response message exceeded the allowed size". Error bodies are only parsed in the format {"error":{"code":..,"message":{"lang":..,"value":..}}} | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/aa74aa8a38f14aa79170832683c7d4d4.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Webhooks are the standard near-real-time outbound push: extension `webhookservices`, a `WebhookConfiguration` = one Integration Object + one Consumed Destination, auto-discovering After Save Events (create/update/delete) on the Integration Object ROOT type, and additionally any event extending `AbstractEvent`. Headers follow the binary CloudEvents spec (ce-source, ce-id, ce-specversion, ce-type, ce-subject = the integration key, optional ce-time and ce-sappassport). At-Least-Once Delivery (ALOD) can be switched on to resend failed messages automatically. In a cluster each instance processes its own webhook events | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/461d8cfe95f5442880491e2cad0a4831.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Outbound Sync publishes new and modified Integration Objects to external systems: extension `outboundsync` on top of `deltadetect` for change detection, configurable per item/when/where, supports POST (create, update) and DELETE and expects HTTP 201 back. It is job-triggered (scheduled), has its own Meta API, and does NOT support polyglot persistence because delta detection is incompatible with it. SAP's three documented outbound options are Outbound Sync (scheduled), Webhooks (near real time) and the Outbound Service Facade (custom code, synchronous) | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/c7ac6fd732944886bfeae734a3604534.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The standard way to restrict an assortment to a customer, customer group or B2B unit is Category Visibility, not an OCC extension: in Backoffice under Catalog / Categories / General / Category Visibility the field "Visible to" takes customers, groups and units ("useful if you want to create multiple product catalogs for various B2B customers"), and front-end enforcement additionally requires activating the SearchRestriction `Frontend_RestrictedCategory` by ImpEx, which SAP ships DEACTIVATED by default for performance reasons. SAP states explicitly that this restriction does NOT apply to search results | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/8c5b1eb586691014a1e6820a672deb23.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `sapcorejco` connection semantics: SAP supports ONLY the service-user scenario, "currently there is no support for the named user scenario", so every JCo call reaches the ABAP system as the technical user of the RFC destination and any customer context must travel as an RFC parameter. Two connection types: Stateless (pooled connection returned to the pool after each function execution, no ABAP context kept) and Stateful (the JCo connection joins the SAP Commerce SESSION and is held open for its duration). Changing certain RFC-destination attributes on a running system invalidates the active JCo connections of user sessions and throws `de.hybris.platform.sap.core.bol.backend.DestinationChangedRuntimeException`; SAP's documented mitigation is error mapping via web.xml or a Spring ExceptionResolver to a page - a JSP-storefront remedy with no OCC equivalent | IEP 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/0af2145522f64abd9d7d00bc69e4745a/8c4c8fb68669101498abeb351b4a7f5e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The public-OCC list on "Securing OCC APIs" has **18 rows**, not the 12 type names usually quoted, and it includes `POST /{baseSiteId}/users`, `POST /{baseSiteId}/orgUsers` and `GET /basesites` next to cms components / cms components/{id} / cms pages / cms pages/{id} / cms sitepages, cardtypes, currencies, languages, titles, countries, countries/{iso}/regions, forgottenpasswordtokens, resetpassword and the two consenttemplates endpoints. So B2C and B2B self-registration stay reachable on an early-login site without any custom annotation. Two SAP notes on the same page: the annotation "is designed to bypass only the early login flag check, but it does not indicate that all other security checks will be skipped", and "registering users still requires a token signed by client ID and secret with grant type client_credentials" - which is in tension with the public-client (no secret) recommendation for the composable storefront and has to be resolved per project | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/243154d31d6145a3a2d6e784ac8e380f.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Early Login granularity: `routing.protected` is **application-level** configuration in the storefront's `ConfigModule`, and SAP states "If the global protected configuration is set to false, all values for individual routes are ignored" - per-route `protected: false` only works underneath a global `protected: true`. There is no per-base-site switch on the storefront half, while the OCC half (`BaseSite.requiresAuthentication`) is per-site data. Closing only some base sites therefore means reconciling two granularities (separate app configurations or deployments per site group, or a custom guard). SAP also recommends guarded CMS components ("Guarding Components") rather than route flags when only a few routes must be protected | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/c190d4cdb1ae4e698f413ec9470fcc17.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The SmartEdit preview contract is storefront-technology-neutral and survives on the composable target: SmartEdit POSTs the experience context to `/previewwebservices/v1/preview`, receives a preview ticket id, and then calls the storefront homepage with `/cx-preview?cmsTicketId=<id>`; the storefront must serve that URL in the requested experience context and keep the context for deep links and direct page access even without the parameter. The preview route is configurable in SmartEdit for custom JS storefronts. So a closed-portal configuration must keep the preview entry point reachable - the requirement carries over even though the JSP-side session preview-ticket check does not | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/3a3bda5d50614f2aa4a299b600281360.html | 2026-09-05 |
| `log4j.deprecated.properties.warn` (default true) and `log4j.deprecated.properties.print` (default false) detect leftover Log4j **1.x configuration properties** (e.g. `log4j.logger.hsqldb.db=warn, CONSOLE`) and print them to the log after a restart. They do NOT detect Log4j 1.x **API usage in Java code** (`org.apache.log4j.Logger.getLogger(...)`), which keeps working through the log4j-1.2-api bridge. Finding code-level Log4j 1.x usage is a source scan, not a platform property | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/b4b7ed2ba3ce40ef8f9d93e95eefba42.html | 2026-09-05 |

| Process engine resilience contract, all platform-managed: `mark.process.as.done.enabled` (default true) disconnects task instances from the business process once the logic succeeded, clears task conditions and sets TaskModel.RUNNERBEAN to passthroughRunner so the task service can remove the task cleanly; action nodes processed in transactions are rerun on connection errors during database outages (`processengine.process.retry.for.connection.failure.during.transaction.enabled`); restart of processes with running nodes is guarded by `processengine.process.restart.retries` / `.millis` / `.exception.if.failed` / `.legacy`; refresh resilience by `processengine.process.refresh.retry.max.tries` / `.retry.delay.ms` / `.failed.task.delay.s`. The page documents NO dedicated business-process cleanup job, so the Data Retention Framework remains the standard mechanism for bounding BusinessProcess and ProcessTask growth | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8c30e9ae86691014a36ed5fd11e24a1e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The vendor documentation itself contradicts `auditing.alltypes.enabled` default **true**: the Generic Audit page's own sample startup message reads "Global auditing enabled: true, auditing for all types enabled: **false**, explicitly enabled types: [...40 entries...]", and the page describes the per-type mechanism as the normal state ("With auditing enabled globally, you can enable it separately for each type you wish to track ... There are multiple item types enabled for auditing by default in the platform configuration"). SAP's sample list has 40 entries, one of which (`conditional.session-cart`) is the change-log filter switch, and the Generic Audit Properties table lists exactly **39 item type names** as `audit.<typeCode>.enabled` default true: user, principalgrouprelation, abstractcontactinfo, address, cart, cartentry, country, currency, order, orderentry, paymentinfo, paymentmode, product, quote, quoteentry, region, title, unit, usergroup, b2bunit, sapb2bdocument, partneraddress, userpasswordchangeaudit, cxusertosegment, cxsegment, btgsegmentresult, btgsegment, consent, consenttemplate, city, district, csticket, comment, employee, csagentgroup, basesite, saporder, customerreview, language. So the documented effective default is global audit ON, all-types OFF, 39 item types audited individually - the properties table's alltypes row is a documentation inconsistency, not the effective behaviour | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/3e2b8491449d43b391eb1ce9c73cbb7f.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/98830906a27446a5b4c5617be2f88175.html | 2026-09-05 |

The Audit Log Storage page adds "For each type, a new corresponding table is created during initialization or update". Consequence: types already carrying the platform's default `audit.<type>.enabled=true` already have their tables and are already filling; the Migrate Data step is required only when the audited SET is extended or when the startup check reports missing tables | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/3e2b8491449d43b391eb1ce9c73cbb7f.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/08a27931a21441b59094c8a6aa2a880e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Cron job abortability: "By default each job is not abortable. To enable the abort feature, you should override the isAbortable method"; alternatively, when extending `AbstractJobPerformable`, override the `abortable` bean property in Spring. Overriding the flag alone is NOT sufficient - SAP states you "can not easily and safely abort a JobPerformable at any time unless you kill the thread", so abort hooks must be placed inside `perform` before each stage expected to be time-consuming. A single long-running SQL statement therefore stays unstoppable even with isAbortable true | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8be670d686691014bdbfac79c33da20e.html | 2026-09-05 |
| SAP's documented first lever against audit database growth is auditing LESS, not deleting more: Change Log Filtering states "Narrowing down audited data to the data that you actually need prevents unnecessary database size growth" and offers two mechanisms - conditional audit (an XML file defining generic filtering configurations, e.g. the shipped rule that audits only saved carts and not session carts) and the `AuditChangeFilter` interface for programmatic filters - next to `auditing.blacklist`, `audit.<typeCode>.blacklistedProperties` and `audit.blacklistedProperties.obfuscationValue` | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/851e92f102324d6cbfb78181d819608e.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/49e2c076602a4798b561eed56519d207.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Generic audit subtype and relation rules: subtypes CANNOT be excluded from an audited supertype ("audit.user.enabled=true plus audit.employee.enabled=false is invalid ... it's impossible to audit Users and at the same time skip Employees"), so the User audit table carries every Customer, Employee and B2BCustomer change; and with many-to-many relations "enabling both sides of the relation isn't enough", the relation type must be configured separately - which is why `principalgrouprelation` appears in the default enabled list in its own right | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/3e2b8491449d43b391eb1ce9c73cbb7f.html | 2026-09-05 |
| There is NO SAP-shipped Extension, module or documentation page named `auditcleanup` at 2211 (help.sap.com search returns no such extension page). | OCC controllers do NOT replace the facade layer - they consume it. "The request comes to commerce web services controller and is in most cases passed directly to commerce facades ... Once the data object is retrieved from the commerce facades, it is converted to WsDTOs (this way web services are isolated from data object changes in the commerce layer)." So project converters, populators and facade beans registered against standard bean ids keep running behind `commercewebservices`; what a composable move adds is the WsDTO mapping on top, and what it removes is the JSP rendering layer, not the facade layer | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/8b422f4b86691014b579bb20d6432cde.html | 2026-09-05 |
| WsDTO Concept: "WsDTO objects have been created initially as closely as possible to commerce services data objects, so mapping is simple and automatically done by Orika mapper", and they are declared as ordinary beans in `commercewebservicescommons-beans.xml`. When adding a WsDTO object "it is recommended to also add a level mapping configuration in the /WEB-INF/config/v2/dto-level-mappings-v2-spring.xml file". Consequence: a facade field with the SAME name needs only a WsDTO bean attribute plus a field-set/level-mapping entry - a `WsDTOMapping`-annotated custom mapper or `AbstractCustomMapper` is required only for renamed, derived or type-converted fields | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/8c8b49a186691014aface4015d79a338.html | 2026-09-05 |
| The Solr `FacetSearchConfig` is served from a platform REGION cache: `cachedFacetSearchConfigService` (alias `facetSearchConfigService`) over `facetSearchConfigCacheRegion` = `FacetSearchConfigCacheRegion` with `facetsearchconfig.cache.maxEntries`, declared in global-solrfacetsearch-spring.xml and monitorable in the HAC. Invalidation is coarse - the ENTIRE cache is cleared whenever a model of a listed type code changes (SolrFacetSearchConfig 2200, SolrIndexedType 2207, SolrIndexedProperty 2208, Language 32, Currency 33, CatalogVersion 601, BaseStore 2013, ...). So a FacetSearchConfig or IndexedType instance obtained from the service is a SHARED object visible to every request and thread on the node; mutating it in a query populator is a cross-request data race, not a per-request customisation | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8b4363f786691014bdfe81fc420cfabe.html | 2026-09-05 |

| An AddOn's `project.properties` is a GENERATED file, not authored configuration: `ant addoninstall` deletes the old one, regenerates it from `project.properties.template` ("the project.properties.template file overwrites the contents of the project.properties file of the AddOn") and writes the line `<storefrontTemplateName>.additionalWebSpringConfigs.<AddOnName>=classpath:/<AddOnName>/web/spring/<AddOnName>-web-spring.xml`. The `addonStorefront.<storefrontTemplateName>` parameter takes a COMMA-SEPARATED list of storefronts, so installing one AddOn into several storefronts is one command and needs no hand edit. On CCv2 the build runs addoninstall from `manifest.json` storefrontAddons, so a hand edit to project.properties that is not in the template is lost on the next build | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/20125f0eca6340dba918bda360e3cdfa/8adf7365866910149ceb975f778d809d.html | 2026-09-05 |
| Role- and user-based visibility of CMS pages and components is standard platform configuration through RESTRICTION TYPES, not through storefront guards: `CMSUserGroupRestriction` and `CMSUserRestriction` are shipped restriction types assigned to page types in Backoffice under WCMS / Page Type / Applicable Restriction Types or by ImpEx on `CmsPageType.restrictionTypes`, SAP's own sample being `;ContentPage;CMSTimeRestriction,CMSUserRestriction,CMSUserGroupRestriction`. | Early Login precision, verbatim: the flag protects "any CMS-driven route (that is, any route that has a CmsPageGuard)", so a custom Angular feature route WITHOUT CmsPageGuard is not covered by `routing.protected` and needs its own `AuthGuard`. SAP states the feature "is not recommended for protecting individual routes, because it only allows you to allowlist public pages. If you want to protect only a small number of routes, it is better to use CMS guarded components instead" - i.e. guarded components are SAP's answer for AUTHENTICATION on a few routes, not for distinguishing one customer type from another. The implementation is `ProtectedRoutesService` (reads the `protected` property from the routing config) and `ProtectedRoutesGuard`, on which `CmsPageGuard` depends - the concrete override point if the flag has to be evaluated per base site at runtime | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/c190d4cdb1ae4e698f413ec9470fcc17.html | 2026-09-05 |

| The standard `patches` extension (`bin/modules/platform/patches`; API classes `AbstractPatch`, `Patch`, `Release`, `StructureState`) is SAP's own OOTB pattern for release-gated ImpEx delivery - "Patches enable you to effectively manage data in a project lifecycle. Patches improve the approach to system maintenance and solve the problem of managing project data across different environments" - and carries no row in either the 2211 or IEP 2211 deprecation status tables, so Patch classes gating per-release ImpEx remain the sanctioned mechanism on CCv2 2211; nothing newer (e.g. a structured "system setup" data-collector) supersedes it for post-go-live release/patch data, as opposed to `@SystemSetup`-driven initial/essential data | 2211 (page last confirmed 2205 in the portal metadata, no deprecation notice at 2211) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE/d0224eca81e249cb821f2cdf45a82ace/2363825c16224da291802709d1627522.html | 2026-09-07 |
| The CCv2 properties precedence table documents two override layers that sit entirely outside the code repository and are applied AFTER build, at deployment time via the Cloud Portal: row 12, "Environment-specific properties defined in static files sets (non-service specific)", and row 13, "Environment-specific common properties defined in the hcs-common service in the Cloud Portal" - both sourced from the customer, both "Applied at Build Time: No", both "Stored At: Deployment time", both "Minimum Action Required to Apply: Restart". They rank after every manifest.json / useConfig persona and persona-plus-aspect property layer (rows 3-11 of the same table), all of which ARE applied at build time - so a repository-only review of any property across `manifest.json` and the `useConfig` persona files can verify only the build-time layer and cannot see or rule out a deployment-time override made through either of these two Cloud Portal mechanisms | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/d090fb3dd48a418d967a1dfdca9fcac6.html | 2026-09-07 |
| `de.hybris.platform.sap.sapordermgmtservices` (incl. its `schedline.data.ScheduleLineData` DTO, populated by `DefaultScheduleLinePopulator` from the BOL `Schedline` business object in the sibling `sapordermgmtbol` extension) is part of the **Synchronous Order Management module** already recorded above as deprecated with 2211 ("This module is not supported for use with SAP Commerce Cloud in the Public Cloud") and **removed from the installer at 2211-jdk21.1**. Public javadoc for this package exists only up to the classic 2211.x (JDK 17) line - pages are dated 2211.0/2211.8/2211.11 - with no equivalent page under the 2211-jdk21 branch, consistent with removal starting at the first jdk21 update. So on 2211-jdk21.17 (or any 2211-jdk21.x >= .1) the extension, the package and `ScheduleLineData` genuinely do not ship under any name - this is not a documentation gap, a renamed extension or an Integration Extension Pack member; the IEP 2211 module catalogue has no Synchronous Order Management module at all. The documented mitigation is module-level only: "Use the SAP S/4HANA Order Management module to create synchronous orders", i.e. `saps4omservices`/`saps4omfacades`/`saps4ombackoffice`/`saps4omocc`, whose own javadoc (`de.hybris.platform.sap.saps4omservices.order.services`, `SapS4OrderManagementConfigService`) is a different package tree with no drop-in class named as a `ScheduleLineData`/schedule-line successor; the closest documented feature-level equivalent is the composable "Estimated Delivery Schedules" capability (library 5.1, back end 2211) that ships on top of the S/4HANA OM integration. | **Two similarly-named but distinct SAP invoice integrations must not be conflated.** (1) The **"SAP Invoice" module** - `sapinvoiceaddon` (AddOn, UI components for B2B stores to view SAP ERP invoices), `sapinvoicebol` (JCo connection to the SAP back end), `sapinvoicefacades`, `sapinvoiceservices` - is the legacy synchronous integration "to enable invoice retrieval from SAP ERP when integrating SAP Commerce Cloud with the SAP back end (SAP S/4HANA or SAP ERP or SAP CRM)"; it has **no OCC member of any kind** and is itself deprecated (Q4 2024, already logged above). (2) The **"SAP Billing Invoice Integration" module** - `sapbillinginvoiceocc`, `sapbillinginvoicefacades`, `sapbillinginvoiceservices` - is a separate, current module whose "Order Invoice" feature retrieves invoice data "using the commercewebservice APIs" (passing `externalSystemId=S4SALES`) and is the one with a genuine composable-storefront front end: the `@spartacus/pdf-invoices` "Invoice Display" library (6.5/2211.12) is documented as "available as part of the SAP S/4HANA Order Management Integration" - i.e. it requires S4OM (`saps4omocc` etc.), not the legacy `sapinvoiceaddon` module. Running `sapinvoiceaddon` (module 1) therefore does not give a ready composable Invoice Display path - that path exists only via module 2 on top of S4OM, a separate back-end-integration decision; the two modules are unrelated in lineage, only similar in end-user purpose | IEP 2211 / composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/04abd17e77b241c79777208fadc6fd7d/9042bc412aa942f4b03f17215909bdd9.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/cebb6a208d8242438b0cb931cd00750f/40efb1b9912549fc94b924eb6183ca29.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/962112809f9a48f9b36aa05b208b3731/47758d87588e4b9f9f3fd638eda68d0f.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **WalkMe Integration is a real, documented composable storefront integration but not a CMS component or an SAP product**: WalkMe is a third-party Digital Adoption Platform: "an overlay ... with interactive guidance, step-by-step walkthroughs, tooltips, on-screen help menus". The documented integration is a one-time bootstrap in the app's root component (`app.component.ts`) that loads the WalkMe loader script and keeps it in sync with language/theme/login state via reactive listeners - it is code wired into the shell, not a CMS-driven component, and it requires the requester's own WalkMe subscription/tenant and content authored on WalkMe's platform (non-developer authoring, outside the CMS). So "WalkMe Integration" is a defensible standard *path* for a guided-tour requirement (floor 2211.29, confirmed in the Feature Compatibility Matrix) but moving a guided tour onto it is a licensing and content-re-authoring decision, not a drop-in CMS component swap | composable storefront 2211.29+ | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/962112809f9a48f9b36aa05b208b3731/b9cb3304c6514675b69f14c9c07fed1e.html | 2026-09-07 |

On the OCC side the enforcing interceptor is likewise named `SecurePortalAuthenticationInterceptor` in `commercewebservicescommons`. | **Version-line equivalence - the key to reading every composable feature floor on a jdk21 estate.** SAP: "Starting with the September 2025 release of composable storefront, the version number changes to 221121.1. This reflects a change in SAP Commerce Cloud, where the September release of SAP Commerce Cloud **2211.44** continues to support JDK 17, and a new, **parallel** September release of SAP Commerce Cloud, version **2211-jdk21.1**, supports JDK 21. The September 2025 release of composable storefront 221121.1 supports both versions of SAP Commerce Cloud." Consequence: a 2211-jdk21.x back end is the parallel of 2211.44+, so every Feature Compatibility Matrix back-end floor quoted on the classic 2211.x scale up to .44 is already met on 2211-jdk21.1 and later - PunchOut 2211.41, WalkMe and Site Theming 2211.29, Multi-Dimensional Products / CAPTCHA / OPPS Login Required 2211.28, Quotes 2211.20, Invoice Display 2211.12, Requested Delivery Date 2211.9.
