| **JCo on CCv2: the documented evidence is one-sided in favour of support, not balanced.** The IEP 2211 Integrations Security Guide states "SAP integrations use HTTP and **JCo RFC connections** between SAP Commerce Cloud, Data Hub, and the SAP NetWeaver Application Server ABAP" as the current mechanism, and scopes SNC explicitly to on-premise: "**On-premise customers must also** set up Secure Network Communication (SNC) for the JCo RFC connections" and "The following Secure Network Communications (SNC) set up is applicable **only to on-premise deployments**" - which presupposes non-on-premise JCo use and matches the CCv2 statement that VPN/NAT or Private Link make SNC unnecessary. Together with `sapcorejco` having no deprecation row at IEP 2211 and both remaining JCo-based modules (SAP Synchronous Pricing, SAP Availability) being current, the sentence "This module is not supported for use with SAP Commerce Cloud in the Public Cloud" must be read as the deprecation reason for the **Synchronous Order Management module only**, not as a statement about JCo as a technology - otherwise the other two JCo modules would carry the same reason, and they do not. A written SAP answer remains worth having for contractual certainty | IEP 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/3c9f66b6aba54f7380a73d1b0ead1563/8c7341fb270a42218e8e26fc91e7145f.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **Deprecated password hashing algorithms are DELETED on the JDK 21 line.** SAP first deprecated and deactivated MD5, salted MD5, PBKDF2, PBKDF2-with-HMAC-SHA1-salted, plain text, SHA-1, SHA-256 and SHA-512 behind `legacy.password.encoding.enabled=false` ("Any attempt to use unsafe password encoders throws an exception in the log"), making **Argon2 the new default algorithm replacing bcrypt**. The deletion was then announced in **2211-jdk21.3** and **implemented in 2211-jdk21.5**: the `legacy.password.encoding.enabled` flag was REMOVED and the encoder classes MD5PasswordEncoder, SaltedMD5PasswordEncoder, PKbasedSaltedMD5PasswordEncoder, SHA1PasswordEncoder, PlainTextPasswordEncoder, PBKDF2WithHmacSHA1SaltedPasswordEncoder plus DeprecatedPasswordEncoder / GenericSaltedPasswordEncoder / SaltedPasswordEncoder were eliminated. SAP states the change touches "User authentication services, Password validation mechanisms, System encryption utilities, Import and export user data process, Login token generation for Remember Me", that "Argon2 remains the default algorithm while bcrypt remains a secure alternative", and that passwords hashed with the removed algorithms "will no longer be verifiable and will require a password reset". Consequence for any estate on 2211-jdk21.5+: an ImpEx row or macro setting `passwordEncoding=md5` (or sha1/plain) references an encoder that no longer exists, and such hashes cannot be migrated by rehashing on the next login | 2211-jdk21.3 (announced) / 2211-jdk21.5 (implemented) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/c00fa1fc70fb44bf94594340ce3022a5.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/0350222591ec46d591f772ee690e43c2.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **B2B Commerce Quotes in the composable storefront - exact adoption shape.** Requirements are composable storefront **2211.20 or higher** and the **`b2bocc`** Extension ("required to run the quote scenario in composable storefront") - there is no separate quote OCC extension. The library is `@spartacus/quote` and installing it "triggers the activation of composable storefront B2B features". The feature is CMS-driven and consists of nine components - QuotesLink, AccountMyQuotesComponent, QuoteHeaderOverviewComponent, QuoteLinksComponent, QuoteItemsComponent, QuoteCommentsComponent, QuoteSummaryComponent, QuoteRequestComponent, QuoteCartGuardComponent - plus two pages (quote list `/my-account/quotes`, quote details `/my-account/quote` on its own QuoteDetailsPageTemplate); SAP publishes the full ImpEx. QuoteCartGuardComponent must be added to the cart slot and to every standard checkout slot (BodyContentSlot-checkout, -checkoutPaymentType, -checkoutDeliveryAddress, -checkoutDeliveryMode, -checkoutReviewOrder, TopContent-cartPage) so that QuoteCartGuard can redirect cart/checkout navigation while a quote is in edit mode. Configuration is `quote.confirmActionDialogMapping` per quote state and action, with role keys **ALL / BUYER / SELLER / SELLERAPPROVER** - so the seller and seller-approver sides are part of the standard storefront feature, not only the buyer side - plus `quote.updateDebounceTime.expiryDate` and `quote.truncateCardTileContentAfterNumChars`. So adopting quotes is a CMS-content and page-template exercise on top of `ng add`, and a project that replaces the standard checkout pages must re-implement the QuoteCartGuard wiring itself | composable storefront 2211.20+ | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/04fb484a1ba34488a79ff23dd541ff88.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **JSP-side Commerce Quotes gate (vendor mechanism).** Commerce Quotes is enabled per site by `site.quotes.enabled.<baseSiteUid>=true` in the storefront extension's project.properties (SAP's own examples: `site.quotes.enabled.powertools=true`, `site.quotes.enabled.apparel-uk=false`), and three ContentPages are **mandatory CMS components** mapped from the controllers: `my-quotes` (/my-account/my-quotes), `quote-detail` (/my-account/my-quotes/{quoteCode}) and `quoteEditPage` (/quote/{quoteCode}/edit). Without that property and those three pages the feature is switched off by the vendor's own documented mechanism | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/402fccfb10da4bc092cc7820bfde3223.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **Automatic Multi-Site Configuration: ONE composable storefront application can serve many base sites.** By simply not defining `context.baseSite` in spartacus-configuration.module.ts, the app fetches the base-site list from the back end before initialisation, compares the current URL with the CMS `urlPatterns` and derives base site, languages, currencies and URL-encoding attributes. The documented request is `GET /rest/v2/basesites?fields=baseSites(uid,defaultLanguage(isocode),urlEncodingAttributes,urlPatterns,**stores**(currencies(isocode),defaultCurrency(isocode),languages(isocode),defaultLanguage(isocode)))` - i.e. **the standard `/basesites` resource does return BASE STORE data** (`baseSites[].stores[]`), and `/basesites` is one of the 18 public OCC endpoints. Three documented caveats: the initial call "can be slow" and must be mitigated by SSR TransferState plus a reverse-proxy cache or by caching the response in the PWA service worker; CMS URL patterns are Java regex evaluated in JavaScript, and a pattern using a Java-only regex feature means the base site is not recognised at all; and the endpoint returns ALL base sites regardless of `active` / `activeFrom` / `activeTo`, so the only documented way to hide a site is to remove its URL patterns. Static Multi-Site Configuration (pinning `context.baseSite`) is the alternative | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/9d2e339c2b094e4f99df1c2d7cc999a8.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **CCv2 property precedence, the LOW end of the same table already cited above: row 1 is `project.properties` of an extension** ("project.properties file in an extension directory", source Commerce Extensions, applied at build time), row 2 default global build-process properties, row 3 manifest global properties, row 4 manifest global properties defined in the `useConfig` customization, rows 5-11 the default and customer aspect/persona layers, rows 12-13 the deployment-time Cloud Portal layers. Consequence: a value set in an Extension's own `project.properties` is the LOWEST-ranked layer and IS overridable per environment through `useConfig` property files, manifest properties or Cloud Portal service properties - the problem with a secret committed there is that it is in the repository and ships in every build, not that it cannot be re-pointed | 2211 (CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/d090fb3dd48a418d967a1dfdca9fcac6.html | 2026-09-07 |

| **RESOLUTION of the `ordermanagementwebservices` row above: the customer-facing OCC return endpoints are declared in `commercewebservices`, and there is NO third webservices gate.** `de.hybris.platform.commercewebservices.core.v2.controller.OrderReturnsController` is a member of the commercewebservices v2 controller package (listed there beside OrdersController, CartsController, SaveCartController, UsersController, ...), declared `@Controller @RequestMapping("/{baseSiteId}/users/{userId}/orderReturns")` with GET list, GET one, POST create and PATCH update; `commercewebservices` has web root `/occ/v2` and `<requires-extension name="commercefacades"/>`. SAP's own OCC guide documents the storefront calls as `POST /occ/v2/{baseSiteId}/users/{userId}/orderReturns` and `POST .../orders/{code}/cancellation`. `ordermanagementwebservices` is a DIFFERENT REST API: it sits in the Order Management module paired with `ordermanagementfacades` ("a simplified interface for ordermanagementwebservices"), exactly as `warehousingwebservices`/`warehousingfacades` and `b2bordermanagementwebservices`/`b2bordermanagementfacades` do, and these appear on the SAP Business Accelerator Hub as their own artifacts (Order Management Webservices B2C/B2B, Warehousing Webservices) separate from Commerce Webservices. Both SAP statements are therefore true and describe different APIs; a `commercewebservices`-hosted return controller is the SHIPPED arrangement, not a project deviation. What a working return path still depends on is the return SERVICE and the BaseStore return-request process code (see the WarehousingReturnService / basecommerce row), not the activation of `ordermanagementwebservices` | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/doc/789b8a6e1434444da824c2493c6faaa0/2211.4/en-us/de/hybris/platform/commercewebservices/core/v2/controller/OrderReturnsController.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/f3a45eae063641f1890b63722c73b862.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/5977842d6547433bb76425b5d2347a52.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **Rehash-on-login is an OPT-IN property, and the password-encoder deletion is documented on the JDK 21 line only.** The "Improved Security of Password Handling" page introduces automatic re-encoding on login as `password.encoding.auto.update.enabled`, **default false**; when set true, a password is rehashed on login if the default algorithm differs from the one in use, or if the same algorithm now has different work-factor parameters (Argon2 memory / parallelism / iterations / saltLength / hashLength, all `password.encoder.argon2.*` variables in core-spring.xml). The same page requires `login.token.encoder` to be checked separately, because the Remember Me cookie login token is encoded with one of these algorithms and must not name a deprecated one. Deprecated algorithm list, verbatim: MD5, salted MD5, PBKDF2, PBKDF2 with HMAC-SHA1 salted, plain text, SHA-1, SHA-256, SHA-512 (SHA-256 and SHA-512 are on the DEPRECATION list but not on the deleted-CLASS list). Version facts: the feature-phases table gives "Feature Activated by Default **2211.28**" and "Feature Available Out-of-the-Box **2211-jdk21.5**"; the deletion note's Version field is **2211-jdk21.5** with revision history 2211-jdk21.3 (announced, 2025-11-04) and 2211-jdk21.5 (implemented, 2025-12-09). **Neither page names a 2211.47 or any other classic-2211.x deletion release** - do not quote one. Deletion consequence, verbatim: "Existing passwords hashed with these current algorithms (Argon2 or bcrypt) remain functional through our re-encoding mechanism. However, passwords previously hashed with the deprecated algorithms being removed will no longer be verifiable and will require a password reset" - so auto-update-on-login cannot rescue an MD5/SHA-1 hash already in the user table on 2211-jdk21.5+, whatever the property is set to, and "bcrypt remains a secure alternative" alongside Argon2 as default | 2211.28 (activated) / 2211-jdk21.5 (deleted) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/0350222591ec46d591f772ee690e43c2.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/c00fa1fc70fb44bf94594340ce3022a5.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **`SAPConfiguration` survives the Synchronous Order Management removal but its `sapordermgmt_*` ATTRIBUTES do not - the type and the attributes have different owning extensions.** The generated javadoc for `de.hybris.platform.sap.core.configuration.model.SAPConfigurationModel` states the type is "first defined at extension `sapcoreconfiguration`" (a current IEP 2211 extension of the Data Hub / SAP core set), while every `sapordermgmt_*` attribute on it is declared elsewhere: SAPORDERMGMT_ENABLED is the "Attribute key of `SAPConfiguration.sapordermgmt_enabled` attribute defined at extension **`sapordermgmtbol`**", and the same for sapordermgmt_dateRange, sapordermgmt_maxHits, sapordermgmt_headerCondTypeFreight, sapordermgmt_sourceFreight and sapordermgmt_sourceNetWOFreight. `sapordermgmtbol` is a named member of the Synchronous Order Management module, which was removed from the installer at 2211-jdk21.1. The same page shows the mechanism in the other direction - `saps4omservices` ADDS `SAPConfiguration.sapS4OrderSubsequentDocumentsActive` in the Type Comparer Report of Modifications - confirming that `SAPConfiguration` is a SHARED type extended per module. Consequence for a 2211-jdk21.x estate: the item type still exists, but `sapordermgmt_enabled` is no longer in the type system, so an `INSERT_UPDATE SAPConfiguration` header carrying that column cannot resolve the attribute descriptor - an ImpEx HEADER failure at import time, not a stale value silently written (how far the failure propagates - the header block or the whole import - depends on the import mode). The database column left behind by an earlier release is orphaned and unaddressable. The one condition under which this softens is a project copy of `sapordermgmtbol` (or any project items.xml re-declaring the attribute) still in the estate's own code - a repository question, not a vendor one | 2211-jdk21.1+ (module removed) / IEP 2211 | https://help.sap.com/doc/cb16fb8fc9af43bd915b2adfeb7d33d9/2211.13/en-US/de/hybris/platform/sap/core/configuration/model/SAPConfigurationModel.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/75d4c3895cb346008545900bffe851ce/23092f338383493d996a944162f4cda1.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/612ca84ab5194f0c89c6216a937bceb0/4e6e2d011bae4840b0080f6f9f86f47f.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **The INTEGRATION EXTENSION PACK has its own parallel release lines, and this is the missing half of the version-line equivalence - IEP floors quoted on the classic 2211.x scale cannot be compared numerically with a `2211-jdk21.x` IEP.** SAP's IEP "Update Releases" table pairs each platform update release with the IEP update release it ships against. On **9 September 2025** the two parallel releases were platform **2211.44** with IEP **2211.38** (JDK 17) and platform **2211-jdk21.1** with IEP **2211-jdk21.1** (JDK 21). So the jdk21 IEP line BRANCHES at the classic IEP 2211.38 mark, and every classic-scale IEP floor at or below 2211.38 - Invoice Display's IEP 2211.11, Requested Delivery Date's IEP 2211.9, S4OM's IEP 2211 - is met by ANY `2211-jdk21.x` IEP, including `2211-jdk21.1`. A plain numeric comparison across the two scales can reach the WRONG answer (e.g. for a 2211-jdk21.1 IEP). | **The 2211-jdk21.5 password-encoder deletion, and why it is a DATA-migration fact rather than a code-path fact.** SAP's release note "Deletion of Deprecated Password Hashing Algorithms": announced in 2211-jdk21.3 (4 November 2025), implemented in **2211-jdk21.5**, Enablement "Automatically on", Valid as of **2025-12-09**, Adopted By **2026-06-09**. It removes the `legacy.password.encoding.enabled` flag and the classes `MD5PasswordEncoder`, `SaltedMD5PasswordEncoder`, `PKbasedSaltedMD5PasswordEncoder`, `SHA1PasswordEncoder`, `PlainTextPasswordEncoder`, `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA1SaltedPasswordEncoder`, plus `DeprecatedPasswordEncoder`, `GenericSaltedPasswordEncoder` and `SaltedPasswordEncoder`. Argon2 stays the default, bcrypt the alternative. SAP states the consequence explicitly: "passwords previously hashed with the deprecated algorithms being removed will no longer be verifiable and will require a password reset". Affected components named by SAP: user authentication services, password validation, system encryption utilities, **the import and export user data process**, and **login token generation for "Remember Me" functionality**. READING RULE: on any estate already past 2211-jdk21.5 this has already happened, so it is not a migration-time event and not a target-API gap - but the two named components make it a live constraint on an authentication rebuild (remember-me tokens) and on any user-data import a migration performs (legacy hashes cannot be carried) | 2211-jdk21.5+ | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/c00fa1fc70fb44bf94594340ce3022a5.html | 2026-09-07 |

| **`secureportaladdon` at 2205: the EXTENSION was deleted, the CAPABILITY was redistributed - and the two must not be conflated in either direction.** SAP's "Upgrading B2B Accelerator from 2105 to 2205" page states verbatim: "The `secureportaladdon` extension has been deprecated and deleted from this release." It then gives the redistribution, which lands in THREE places, not one: (1) "All B2B customer registration code has been moved from the `secureportaladdon` extension to `b2bcommerce` and `b2bcommercefacades` extensions" (dao, email, jalo, model, services, workflows, facades, exceptions, testsrc, impex, processes all deleted from a project copy); (2) "Move following files from `secureportaladdon` to `b2bacceleratoraddon`" - the storefront web layer: `acceleratoraddon/web/src/.../controllers`, `forms`, `interceptors`, `services`, the responsive tags and page views, and `_ui/responsive/common/js/secureportal.js` plus its `b2bacceleratoraddon.javascript.paths.responsive` entry; (3) from `secureportaladdon-items.xml` delete the itemtypes `CMSSecurePortalRestriction`, `CMSSite` ("The attributes `requiresAuthentication` and `enableRegistration` have been moved to the `BaseSite`"), `B2BRegistration`, `B2BRegistrationProcess`, `B2BRegistrationApprovedProcess`, `B2BRegistrationRejectedProcess`. At 2211 `CMSSecurePortalRestrictionModel` is "first defined at extension `b2bacceleratorservices`" and B2B Early Login is documented under `b2bacceleratoraddon`. So the correct formulation for any analysis is: the extension was deleted at 2205, the capability survives at 2211, and `BaseSite.requiresAuthentication` is the attribute SAP itself moved off `CMSSite` in that step - which independently corroborates the Early Login (`routing.protected` + `BaseSite.requiresAuthentication`) closed-portal successor rather than a CMS restriction type | 2205 (deletion) / 2211 (successor state) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bbcd2978537e4ae298479557c0b8fdbd/69c0d5d94a5942b3a56326003b323613.html?version=v2205 and https://help.sap.com/doc/c14b940047cf4dfeb4332e93d8afad8f/2211/en-us/de/hybris/platform/cms2/model/contents/class-use/CMSItemModel.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/8ac304ca866910148e908988466c0bd7.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **B2B Reorder is CMS-driven content work per content catalogue, with no documented extension point and a cart-replacing behaviour.** SAP: "The reorder feature is CMS driven, and consists of the `AccountOrderDetailsReorderComponent` CMS component", enabled by installing `@spartacus/order` and then either using SmartEdit or adding it by ImpEx - `INSERT_UPDATE CMSFlexComponent` with `flexType=AccountOrderDetailsReorderComponent`, then `UPDATE ContentSlot` appending it to `BodyContent-orderdetail`, against one `$contentCatalog` at a time. The page adds "No special configuration is required" AND "No special extensibility is available for this feature", and documents the behaviour as: "the active cart is replaced by a new cart that contains only the items from the order you are viewing on the Order Details page. Any items that were previously in your cart are effectively removed." Consequence: with one content catalogue per country, adopting standard Reorder costs one CMS component seeded per catalogue plus acceptance of an always-replace cart, i.e. the same adoption shape as B2B Commerce Quotes and the composable Cancellations-and-Returns UI - adoptable, but not free | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/d4391e3e54434303b0ec502d3ff7e32b.html | 2026-09-07 |
| **`default.password.encoding` is the documented default-encoder property, and the `$passwordEncoding` / `$setPassword` ImpEx macro is SAP'S OWN published idiom.** The 2211 Security Guide "Password Encoding" page ranks the supported strategies "Argon2: the strongest algorithm / scrypt: a strong algorithm, recommended if you can't use Argon2 / bcrypt: a strong algorithm, kept for backward compatibility", states the default is Argon2 and "When necessary, you can change it through the `default.password.encoding` property", and shows the switch to bcrypt as the worked example. The same page publishes the ImpEx pattern verbatim with **bcrypt** as the sample value: `$passwordEncoding=bcrypt` and `$setPassword=@password[translator=de.hybris.platform.impex.jalo.translators.ConvertPlaintextToEncodedUserPasswordTranslator][default='$passwordEncoding:$defaultPassword']`. Two consequences for reading an estate: a project file carrying that macro with a different algorithm is a copied SAP sample with the value changed, and `ConvertPlaintextToEncodedUserPasswordTranslator` is CURRENT at 2211 - so what a removed algorithm breaks is the VALUE, not the mechanism. Note also that the 2211 security guide's wording for bcrypt ("kept for backward compatibility") is weaker than the later deletion note's ("bcrypt remains a secure alternative"); the deletion note is the newer statement | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/ddc1ef9fc7fa48cb90f2b57c061a4012/cdf5af8d1fdf428ca10df6084d4a60b6.html | 2026-09-07 |

| **The documented route for putting a field onto an OCC payload is FOUR steps with two hard conditions - not "a field-set entry".** SAP's "Extending Data Objects" page (the yocc/xyzocc worked example) prescribes, in order: (1) re-declare the vendor FACADE DATA OBJECT in the project `-beans.xml` with `extends` and add the property (`<bean class="de.hybris.platform.commercefacades.user.data.CustomerData" extends="...PrincipalData"><property name="nickname" type="String"/>`); (2) write a `Populator` and register it on the EXISTING converter through the `modifyPopulatorList` parent bean - "It is possible to add a Populator to an existing Converter without having to redeclare the Converter" - with SAP's own follow-up "The same actions must be performed to the reverse Converter"; (3) re-declare the WsDTO the same way (`<bean class="de.hybris.platform.commercewebservicescommons.dto.user.UserWsDTO" extends="...PrincipalWsDTO">`); (4) add the field to the predefined field level configuration with a `fieldSetLevelMapping` parent bean whose BASIC / DEFAULT / FULL map decides whether the field appears in a response at all. CONDITION A (placement, hard): that `-web-spring.xml` must live in an extension whose NAME ENDS IN `occ`, at `/resources/occ/v2/<*occ>/web/spring/`, and its file name must end in `-web-spring.xml`, because `commercewebservices` loads OCC extension beans through the single import `<import resource="classpath*:/occ/v2/*occ/web/spring/*-web-spring.xml"/>` - a file elsewhere is silently never loaded. CONDITION B (mapping): Orika maps SAME-NAMED fields automatically via `DataMapper`; a renamed, derived or type-converted field needs a `WsDTOMapping`-annotated mapper or `AbstractCustomMapper`. CONSEQUENCE for sizing any "extend the standard OCC payload" item: it is four documented extension points across facade and OCC layers plus, where the data is not yet on the facade object, whatever retrieval hook the source module publishes - cheap and standard, but not one line | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/4d1e88e78fff4e12bdd1cf82e7cfebcf.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/8ef2d8d7baf1443dbed3affb49606adf.html | 2026-09-07 |

| **`/basesites` caching, SAP's exact wording - the page is PERMISSIVE, not prescriptive, and the one-day figure is an example.** Verbatim: "The initial call to the back end for base sites can be slow, which affects the user experience. To address this, **you can choose to** cache the context using server-side rendering (SSR) or progressive web application (PWA) techniques." SSR route: identify the site during SSR and transfer the context with Angular `TransferState`, and "To avoid making calls for base sites on the server side with every page request, the pages can be cached using a reverse proxy." PWA route: cache the response in the Angular service worker by adding a `dataGroups` entry to `ngsw-config.json`, whose published example carries `"maxAge": "1d", // Set to 1 day. Customize this value according to your needs`, followed by "In the example above, the maxAge shows a value of one day. **You can customize this value according to your needs.**" Active-site behaviour, verbatim: "The back end endpoint returns a list of all base sites, without any information about whether the site is active or not, regardless of the options defined in the CMS, such as active, activeFrom, or activeTo. To disable a base site, you must remove the URL patterns for that base site", with a named "alternative, low-level workaround" of restricting the back-end database call to active sites. So SAP documents caching as a permissive mitigation, not a requirement, and one day is the documented example value | composable storefront 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/9d2e339c2b094e4f99df1c2d7cc999a8.html | 2026-09-07 |

| **An ImpEx header carrying a column the type system cannot resolve is a DOCUMENTED HEADER-LEVEL ERROR, not a silently ignored column.** SAP's "Analyzing Import Results for Errors" page defines two specialisations of `ImpExError`: `ImpExHeaderError`, of which SAP says verbatim "Header related errors are present only if a particular header is invalid (for instance, contains an invalid column name, invalid type code, etc.)"; and `ImpExValueLineError`, whose `ImpExLineErrorType` enum contains `WRONG_OR_MISSING_HEADER` alongside PARTIALLY_PROCESSED / NOT_PROCESSED / PARTIALLY_PROCESSED_WITH_ERROR / NOT_PROCESSED_WITH_ERROR / NOT_PROCESSED_CONFLICTING / UNKNOWN, and SAP notes value-line errors "are present always for any dumped and not resolved lines - even if they have been partially processed". So an unresolvable attribute column fails AT THE HEADER and the value lines beneath it are dumped rather than written. Vendor documentation does NOT state how far the failure propagates beyond that header block - that depends on the import mode / dump handling and must be stated as a boundary, not a fact | 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/9375fcee679048da9e1dc7f580cb0ed9.html | 2026-09-07 |

| SAML SSO INTO THE COMPOSABLE STOREFRONT IS STANDARD, CONFIGURED FUNCTIONALITY ON THE JDK 21 LINE - NOT A CUSTOM OAUTH2 INTEGRATION. The Custom Login Page page carries a section 'Configuring SAML SSO as the Login Page': 'You can use the loginPageUri attribute to integrate SAML Single Sign-On into the standard Authorization Code plus Proof Key for Code Exchange (PKCE) flow for Single-Page Application (SPA) clients, such as Composable Storefront.' Mechanism, verbatim: an unauthenticated SPA hits /authorizationserver/oauth/authorize, Spring Security saves the full request in the HTTP session and redirects to the configured loginPageUri; the samlsinglesignon extension performs the SAML round-trip with the identity provider and writes a login token cookie; the authorization server validates the cookie, establishes the security context and redirects back to /oauth/authorize. Configuration is exactly two items: samlsinglesignon added to the 'api' aspect webapps in manifest.json (contextPath /samlsinglesignon), and OAuthClientDetails.loginPageUri set to the relative URL '/samlsinglesignon/saml/authorizationserver/login' (Backoffice or ImpEx). The Single Sign-On overview page adds that this supports 'SAML-authenticated SPA users without requiring any frontend changes'. Azure Active Directory has its own documented identity-provider page in the same guide. This page is JDK-21-only ('This page contains information about functionality that is only relevant if you're using JDK 21'). | 2211-jdk21 / composable storefront 221121.x | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/ca1176a372b242a6abd75a39fe803eea.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-005 |

| NEGATIVE FINDING, STATED SO IT IS NOT RE-RESEARCHED: SAP PUBLISHES NO PRICES, NO LICENSING METRIC AND NO SUBSCRIPTION SCOPE IN THE SANCTIONED DOCUMENTATION DOMAINS. Targeted searches of the SAP Commerce Cloud documentation on 2026-09-17 for license price, subscription cost, order or revenue metric, and maintenance percentage return only functional pages (subscription PRODUCT modelling, pricing of goods) and no commercial terms. What the documentation does say is that the commercial terms live in the contract and with sales, three times over: 'Some of these offerings are free and others are paid. In case of doubt, please contact your SAP Commerce Cloud sales representative' (Complementary Products); 'An SAP Commerce Cloud extension may provide functionality that is licensed through different SAP Commerce Cloud modules. Make sure to limit your implementation to the features defined in your contract license. In case of doubt, please contact your sales representative' (extension pages, e.g. yacceleratorstorefront); and the Feature Scope Description is bound to the GTC rather than to a price list. Consequence: every licence band, TCO figure, maintenance percentage, contract term and implementation-cost range quoted for this product is UNVERIFIABLE from vendor documentation by construction. Such figures may only be used as questions to the client or as sourced third-party estimates, never as vendor-documented facts, and no engagement claim may cite this reference file for them. | SAP Commerce Cloud commercial terms (searched 2026-09-17) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/12be4ac419604b01aabb1adeb2c4c8a2/24fc607154ce4317bdebf85dce87bf07.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-011 |
| THE INTEGRATION EXTENSION PACK IS A SEPARATELY VERSIONED BUILD ARTIFACT, DECLARED IN manifest.json AND VERSION-PAIRED WITH THE PLATFORM INDEPENDENTLY. The Extension Packs page documents the mechanism: 'Extension packs allow you to identify more versioned software artifacts to combine with SAP Commerce Cloud during installation. Extension packs are downloaded during the build process'. For the integration extension pack the documented manifest shape uses the Maven coordinates form - commerceSuiteArtifact 'de.hybris.platform.suite:commerce-suite:<version>' plus extensionPacks entry artifact 'de.hybris.platform.suite:commerce-integrations:<version>' - and every extension actually used must additionally be listed in the 'extensions' array. The IEP version is NOT the platform version: the Productive Update Releases table pairs platform 2211-jdk21.18 with IEP 2211-jdk21.15 and platform 2211-jdk21.17 with IEP 2211-jdk21.13 (see the release-inventory row in this section). Practical consequence for reading a client manifest: the presence or absence of an extensionPacks block, and the IEP version inside it, are separate facts from commerceSuiteVersion, and an IEP extension can only be in the build if both the pack is declared and the extension is listed. | 2211 (CCv2 manifest) / IEP 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/ad98c976ab3d433e935b4b5c89303dd5.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-012 |
| THE CCv2 BUILD DOES NOT EXECUTE THE PLATFORM 'customize' STEP - A hybris/config/customize FOLDER IS INERT IN THE CLOUD BUILD. The 2211 page 'Replacing OOTB Files' (Code Repository Setup and Build Guidelines) states verbatim: 'SAP Commerce Cloud implements a fixed build process, so you can't run [the customize target] or change files inside a Commerce release. However, you can replace them at build time to override default configuration or functionality - for example, to fix a bug before a patch is available. This process requires you to create a custom extension and include it in the repository you will use to build your instance of Commerce.' The ONLY sanctioned CCv2 mechanism for replacing a delivered file is a macrodef <ext>_before_build in the custom extension's buildcallbacks.xml that deletes the original and copies the replacement using ${ext.<name>.path} properties; SAP adds that 'replacing out-of-the-box files isn't recommended because of the possibility of damaging the build'. Consequence: files under core-customize/hybris/config/customize take effect only in a LOCAL or on-premise build; in the CCv2 build they are dead weight, not an override of the standard, unless the same replacement is also wired through buildcallbacks.xml. | 2211 (CCv2 build process) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/18d4f933b3fb47a19027fbca15a71b01.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-013 |
| THE COCKPIT DELETION IS DATED AND ITS LIST IS CLOSED: EIGHT EXTENSIONS, REMOVED IN 2211-jdk21.1, VALID AS OF 2025-09-09. The What's New entry 'Deletion of Deprecated Cockpit Extensions' reads 'the deprecated cockpit related extensions have been removed in this release' and its Technical Details give Lifecycle Phase 'Deleted', Valid as Of 2025-09-09, Version 2211-jdk21.1, Adopted By 2026-08-31. The removed set is exactly: cockpit, admincockpit, cmscockpit, productcockpit, configurablebundlecockpits, subscriptioncockpits, subscriptionbundlecockpits, yacceleratorcockpits. The companion page 'Deprecated Cockpit Extensions - Deletion in SAP Commerce Cloud 2211' names the same eight (announced for Q3 2025), records that they were deprecated in 6.6 or 6.7, and gives the paths forward: Backoffice for cockpit/admincockpit/productcockpit/configurablebundlecockpits/subscriptioncockpits/subscriptionbundlecockpits, SmartEdit for cmscockpit, yacceleratorbackoffice for yacceleratorcockpits. Reason given: the cockpits are built on Spring 5.x and JDK 17 and are incompatible with the Spring 6 framework update. TWO READING RULES. (1) cscockpit and reportcockpit are NOT on this list and are NOT rows in the 2211 Deprecation Status table either, so their absence from a 2211-jdk21 delivery must be evidenced from the delivery tree or from an earlier release note - it cannot be cited to this page. (2) The deletion is a DELIVERY fact with a release number: 'these cockpit extensions no longer exist' is precise for 2211-jdk21.1 and later and does not need to be argued from the tree. | 2211-jdk21.1 (cockpit deletion) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/58a4ff4e9d304a04a5ae809a024da902.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/9b5366ff6eb34df5be29881ff55f97d2/eed83e2b3de8482d9c2c7d283778691a.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-014 |
| THE 2211 DEPRECATION STATUS TABLE READ ROW BY ROW: ONLY TWO DELETION DATES EXIST IN THE ACCELERATOR/OCC BLOCK, EVERYTHING ELSE SAYS 'No date'. Columns are Deprecated Component / Module / Announced deprecation with / Planned deletion. Q1 2027 (the OCC AddOn family, all announced with 2205): acceleratorwebservicesaddon (core-accelerator), cmsoccaddon, b2boccaddon, savedorderformsoccaddon, notificationoccaddon, messagecentercsoccaddon, customerinterestsoccaddon, customercouponoccaddon, consignmenttrackingoccaddon, marketplaceoccaddon, timedaccesspromotionengineoccaddon and the five chinese*occaddon rows. Q3 2027: ycommercewebservices and ycommercewebservicestest (commerce-services) - i.e. the OCC v2 TEMPLATE extensions - plus hybrisanalyticsaddon. Q4 2028: the four eventtracking* rows (announced 2011). 'To be determined': y2ysync, y2ysyncbackoffice (announced 1905). Every other row carries 'No date', including yacceleratorstorefront, acceleratorstorefrontcommons, addonsupport, b2bacceleratoraddon, commerceorgaddon, commerceorgsamplesaddon, smarteditaddon, assistedservicestorefront, customerticketingaddon, orderselfserviceaddon, captchaaddon, yaddon, b2bpunchoutaddon, accountsummaryaddon, consignmenttrackingaddon, marketplaceaddon, selectivecartaddon, stocknotificationaddon and the whole personalization*/chinese*/assistedservice* AddOn set. The mitigation text on every Accelerator row is identical and says maintenance continues 'until such time as they are removed (for which there is currently no date set)'. THREE READING RULES. (1) 'SAP ships it in a deprecated/ folder' and 'SAP has dated its removal' are different facts: of a typical loaded Accelerator set only acceleratorwebservicesaddon carries a date. (2) The Q3 2027 date belongs to SAP's own template extension ycommercewebservices; a customer extension GENERATED from that template is not itself removed by SAP on that date - what happens is that the template and its AddOns stop being shipped and maintained, so the generated copy is left unsupported. (3) A statement 'deprecated, planned deletion <date>' must be sourced per extension from this table, never derived from the module it belongs to. | 2211 (Deprecation Status table, read 2026-09-17) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/12be4ac419604b01aabb1adeb2c4c8a2/8bb15ed586691014a948d1553f4947cf.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-015 |
| THE MANIFEST SCHEMA SETTLES THE PERSONA QUESTION: PROPERTIES ARE PERSONA-SCOPED, THE EXTENSION LIST IS NOT. The 2211 Manifest Components Reference lists under useConfig exactly three children for the extension list - useConfig.extensions (object, 'The reference to the existing localextensions.xml file'), useConfig.extensions.location (string, required, 'The location of the localextensions.xml file ... path must be relative to the core-customize directory and in Unix format') and useConfig.extensions.exclude (array of strings, 'The extensions to exclude (not imported from the localextensions.xml file)'). There is NO persona attribute anywhere under useConfig.extensions. A persona attribute exists only on useConfig.properties[x].persona ('The environment persona for which the properties are applied'), properties[x].persona and aspects[x].properties[y].persona. CONSEQUENCE, and it is a hard one: in CCv2 there is exactly ONE set of loaded extensions for development, staging and production - environment differentiation is possible for PROPERTIES only. A sample-data, test-data or tooling extension listed in localextensions.xml therefore runs in production too, and the only lever left is to switch its behaviour off by property. The same page documents solrVersion as 'The Solr version expressed by two numbers separated with a dot, for example 8.6' and the top-level extensions array as 'A list of extensions to be enabled'; the companion page 'Selecting Solr Server Version' adds that the build validates the requested version against the supported set and picks the most up-to-date base image, and that this setting does not affect the solrfacetsearch client or the solrserver extension shipped in the Commerce zip. | 2211 (CCv2 manifest schema) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/3d562b85b37a460a92d32ec991459133.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-016 |
| THE OCC-ADDON REMOVAL PAGE NAMES ITS STAGES BY CATEGORY AND PUBLISHES NO EXTENSION LIST - AND THE DEPRECATION STATUS TABLE CARRIES NO Q3 2026 ROW AT ALL. Read in full on 2026-09-17. The gradual schedule is given verbatim as four category lines only ('IEP and Industry OCC as well as template AddOns' Q3 update of 2026; 'Chinese Accelerator and Core OCC AddOns' Q1 update of 2027; 'Template Extensions' Q3 update of 2027; 'Code associated with OCC v1' removed in 2211.16) - nowhere on the page is a single extension named for the Q3-2026 stage. Cross-checked against the 2211 Deprecation Status table: the only Planned deletion values that occur anywhere in that table are Q3 2024 (three financial-services-accelerator rows), Q1 2027 (16 rows, the OCC AddOn family), Q3 2027 (ycommercewebservices, ycommercewebservicestest, hybrisanalyticsaddon) and Q4 2028 (four eventtracking rows), plus 'To be determined' and 'No date'. CONSEQUENCE, and it cuts both ways: a POSITIVE membership statement for the Q3-2026 stage cannot be sourced from SAP, and neither can a NEGATIVE one - any membership statement for that stage is an inference from a naming rule that must be stated, not a documented fact. TWO FURTHER FACTS FROM THE SAME PAGE. (1) The outer wall is dated and it is a BUILD block, not merely an end of support: 'starting September 2028, no new builds are possible if using the update release of SAP Commerce Cloud that contains the previous Java version, which also includes the deprecated AddOns and extensions'; after the September 2027 removal the AddOns remain available as a separate download but 'won't be scanned for security issues nor receive updates'. (2) The page defines the module boundary: 'The commercewebservices module represents core REST APIs for SAP Commerce Cloud, including the most important ycommercewebservices extension. This module includes the OCC v2 templates and shares common code with OCC AddOns.' READ THIS WITH THE EXTENSION MAP: B2B organisation management is NOT in commercewebservices - the b2bocc extension 'exposes saved functionality of the B2B Accelerator Module in the form of the standardized OCC API' from bin/modules/b2b-accelerator, with endpoints such as GET /{baseSiteId}/orgUsers/{userId}/orgUnits, and cXML punchout is b2bpunchoutocc. Neither b2bocc nor b2bpunchoutocc appears in the Deprecation Status table, while b2boccaddon does (Q1 2027) - i.e. the non-AddOn OCC extensions are the surviving standard surface and must be named individually in any target-standard statement. | 2211 (OCC AddOn removal stages; b2bocc / b2bpunchoutocc) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/3476714bba0b4cb9b3eb58c270e44439/4a4f5d0d120340fc837106e68b894d74.html and https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/7e47d40a176d48ba914b50957d003804/4b3f9603358e4c4e906fd1f0f0fae684.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-018 |
| JSP AND TAG FILES OF AN ACCELERATOR STOREFRONT HAVE A DOCUMENTED, NON-DESTRUCTIVE OVERRIDE ROUTE - SO REPLACING A DELIVERED .jsp OR .tag FILE AT BUILD TIME IS NEVER THE ONLY OPTION. The 2211 page 'Customizing the Storefront Using an AddOn' states verbatim: 'AddOns override the default configuration of the storefront so that it appears or behaves as desired, without the need to customize extensions', and the documented procedure is to create an AddOn, 'Copy the storefront files that you wish to customize. The folder should have the same contents as the target storefront folder', and then change 'JSP Files / Impex Files / Tag Files' plus, where applicable, Spring and Controller configuration; installation is per storefront ('Installing an AddOn for a Specific Storefront'). CONSEQUENCE FOR READING A buildcallbacks.xml REPLACEMENT LIST: the replacements must be split by file kind. A delivered .jsp or .tag file replaced through a <ext>_before_build macro has a sanctioned alternative that touches nothing in the delivery - the AddOn override. A delivered .java class does not: there the sanctioned route is the platform's normal extension mechanism, a subclass plus a bean alias in the extension's spring configuration, never shadowing or replacing the delivered class file. This makes the SAP caution on 'Replacing OOTB Files' (see the row on the CCv2 build and the customize step) sharper rather than softer: for .jsp and .tag files the product documents a clean route. Note the counterweight: the AddOn mechanism itself (addonsupport, and the AddOns of the Accelerator UI) is deprecated since 2205 with planned deletion 'No date', so an AddOn override is the right move only while the JSP storefront exists at all and is never a target-architecture answer. | 2211 (Accelerator AddOn storefront override) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/20125f0eca6340dba918bda360e3cdfa/8ad05d17866910148d06e408f617dd40.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-019 |
| MULTI-COUNTRY / MULTI-MARKET SUPPORT IS A DATA-DRIVEN, SEPARATE-BASESITE-PER-MARKET PATTERN, NOT A URL-ENCODED-ATTRIBUTE PATTERN. The 2211 'Technical Overview of Multi-Country Site Support' page states the feature is 'native support for managing sites that consist of multiple content catalogs and that use content inheritance' and that 'the enablement of the feature is data driven'. Its worked example creates one BaseSite per market/region (Electronics, Electronics EU, Electronics DE, Electronics UK, Electronics US), each with its own content-catalog chain that inherits from and overrides its parent; a page not overridden in a market's own catalog falls back to the parent catalog's page. This is the vendor-documented route for 'add a new sales organisation / market without a code change' on BOTH the Accelerator and the composable storefront (the BaseSite/catalog model is frontend-independent) - the mechanism is a new BaseSite plus catalog data, never a single site with a country value encoded into the URL path. Consequence: a requirement for code-free market/sales-org creation is met by this standard BaseSite pattern. | 2211 (Multi-Country Site Support; BaseSite/catalog model, frontend-independent) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/9b6a43313c3e4587b664370cb94391a5.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-021 |
| TENANTAWARETHREADFACTORY IS THE DOCUMENTED, NAMED MECHANISM FOR TENANT-SAFE CUSTOM THREAD POOLS. The dedicated help page 'TenantAwareThreadFactory' states verbatim: a plain ThreadFactory's threads are not aware of the tenant context in which the creating object was initialized; to have a thread with access to the tenant session, use TenantAwareThreadFactory (class de.hybris.platform.core.TenantAwareThreadFactory), whose run() method activates a JaloSession/tenant before the runnable and deactivates it afterward. The page gives a worked example for wiring it into a Spring ThreadPoolTaskScheduler via constructor-arg tenant=tenantFactory, because Spring's own scheduler thread factory is documented as NOT tenant-aware by default. Consequence: the statement that the platform provides no tenant-aware thread-pool factory is false for 2211 - a named class exists and is the documented fix; a custom thread pool without it is a documented anti-pattern, not an unavoidable gap. | 2211-jdk21 (core platform, all versions) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/6a782448755941d5adb22b8e39664a2b.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-022 |
| THE 2211 DEPRECATION STATUS TABLE CARRIES NO ROW FOR JALO OR THE LEGACY/JALO LAYER - CHECKED BY FULL-TEXT SEARCH, NOT BY SAMPLING. A direct text search of the full rendered Deprecation Status table page for '2211' (the same page already read row-by-row for the Accelerator/OCC block, see the row on that block in this section) for the string 'jalo' (case-insensitive) returns zero matches. Consequence: 'the Jalo/legacy layer carries no deprecation-list entry for 2211' is a confirmed negative finding, not an inference from the Accelerator/OCC subset. | 2211 (Deprecation Status table, checked 2026-09-17) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/12be4ac419604b01aabb1adeb2c4c8a2/8bb15ed586691014a948d1553f4947cf.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-023 |
| EXTERNAL-DEPENDENCIES.XML IS THE DOCUMENTED, SANCTIONED ROUTE FOR THIRD-PARTY LIBRARY VERSIONS; A PLAIN JAR UNDER /lib WITHOUT ONE BYPASSES THE PLATFORM'S OWN VERSION/DUPLICATE/LICENSE SCAN. The 'Extension Components' page states verbatim that third-party dependencies of SAP Commerce Cloud extensions are defined in maven pom.xml-style files called external-dependencies.xml (root directory for a coremodule, web/webroot/WEB-INF for a webmodule); an extension MAY also simply drop jars into /lib ('coremodule ... contains libraries that are required for the extension itself to function ... may also be used by other extensions'), but the page then describes a distribution-time process that packages every extension's external-dependencies.xml into a combined dependencies zip which a tool ('VersionEye') checks 'to ensure that everything is OK on the SAP Commerce Cloud level', explicitly 'to give early feedback about outdated libraries and invalid software licenses' and 'it is important to check for duplicates or different versions of the same library'. Consequence: a jar dropped only under /lib with no external-dependencies.xml entry is a documented, legal way to ship a library, but it is invisible to the platform's own outdated-library/duplicate/license tooling - such library versions are unmanaged by that tooling. | 2211-jdk21 (extension component / library management, all versions) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/20125f0eca6340dba918bda360e3cdfa/3a3b92d4900b4b3685157b806a73eab2.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-024 |
| EXTEND-AND-OVERRIDE-A-DELIVERED-SERVICE-CLASS, WIRED THROUGH SPRING, IS THE SAP-DOCUMENTED MECHANISM FOR CHANGING PLATFORM LOGIC - AND THE DOCUMENTATION NEVER WARNS ABOUT NON-PUBLIC MEMBERS OF THE BASE CLASS. The 'Changing the Logic of an Existing Service' tutorial states verbatim the canonical pattern: 'you simply extend the Default<X>Service and add your logic... you override an existing [method]', then wire the subclass in place of the delivered bean. This is the same mechanism a bean-alias override in a later-loaded extension's Spring XML implements (redefine the delivered bean id, or repoint its alias, to a subclass). CONSEQUENCE FOR RISK ASSESSMENT: subclassing a delivered class to change its behaviour is itself the sanctioned, textbook approach - not a deviation - so a disposition or depth rationale may not treat the override mechanism itself as the risk. What the tutorial and every adjacent extensibility page never document or warn against is a subclass that needs package-private or private members of the delivered class to reproduce delivered behaviour: that dependency is real (Java enforces it silently as a compile-time constraint, so there is no build warning), but it is a property of the SPECIFIC delivered class's chosen visibility, not of the extension mechanism, and SAP publishes no per-class contract about which members remain accessible across releases. A claim correctly separates these: 'we override a delivered service' is standard and unremarkable; 'we override it by depending on non-public members, which future SAP releases may change or remove without notice' is the real, correctly-scoped risk. | 2211-jdk21 (extending/changing delivered service logic, all versions) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/465a25442fd64a1ab33d98362d66d25b.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-025 |
| OCC IS NOT LITERALLY STATELESS: SAP DOCUMENTS SESSION COOKIES AND SESSION AFFINITY AS PART OF THE OCC ARCHITECTURE, SO 'HEADLESS = NO SERVER SESSION' OVERSTATES THE TARGET. The page 'Route Cookies, Session Cookies, and Sticky Sessions in SAP Commerce Cloud' states verbatim: 'Session cookies ensure that subsequent requests are associated with the same session state... Even in stateless APIs in SAP Commerce Cloud, there are occasions when multiple parallel or sequential calls could fail' (the add-to-cart / load-cart race example), and recommends API clients resend the received route cookie. Consequence for reading a claim that says a server-session-based feature 'breaks because the target interface is stateless': OCC calls a cart-bearing session 'stateless APIs' only loosely - session state for cart/user continuity is real and server-side, coordinated via session and route cookies, not eliminated. The precise, defensible version of a 'this breaks under headless' finding for a feature built on javax.servlet HttpSession attributes is not 'no session exists', but 'no equivalent filter/interceptor runs in the OCC/API webapp pipeline to populate or consume that attribute, and Spartacus/OCC clients have no reason to carry it' - an absent hook, not a statelessness violation. | 2211-jdk21 / OCC (session and route cookies) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/20125f0eca6340dba918bda360e3cdfa/734e8c6cfee44838b2abe0e59b63641a.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-026 |
| THE extensioninfo.xsd 'managersuperclass' ATTRIBUTE DEFAULTS TO de.hybris.platform.jalo.extension.Extension FOR EVERY EXTENSION, REGARDLESS OF ITEMTYPES. The 2211 'Extension Configurations' schema reference for extensioninfo.xml documents the root <extension> element's managersuperclass attribute verbatim: 'Class name of the manager's superclass. Default is de.hybris.platform.jalo.extension.Extension.' This default applies to every generated/declared extension whether or not it defines any itemtype, bean, process, cronjob or ImpEx. Consequence for reading a Jalo-deprecation heuristic across an estate: a DEP-JALO hit on an extensioninfo.xml line that only carries this attribute (explicit or ant-generated default) is boilerplate, not evidence of Jalo business logic; the itemtypes/beans/processes actually declared by the extension are the real signal. | 2211 (extensioninfo.xsd schema, all releases) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/20125f0eca6340dba918bda360e3cdfa/8bbf6b5e86691014ad49fd1bc0172e5c.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-027 |
| THE CCv2 'admin' ASPECT IS HEADLESS AND CANNOT DEFINE ANY WEBAPP - hac IS NOT A DEFAULT WEBAPP OF THE admin ASPECT. The 2211 'Aspects' page states, for the admin aspect: it performs administration tasks such as initialization and updatesystem, and in the Webapps Aspect section: 'The administration aspect can't define any web application because it's intended to be headless (no Tomcat server is started)', with its default-webapps row reading 'admin: not applicable'. The documented DEFAULT webapps per aspect (used when no webapps are declared in manifest.json) are: backgroundProcessing -> hac, mediaweb; accstorefront -> mediaweb, acceleratorservices; backoffice -> backoffice, hac, mediaweb; admin -> not applicable (none). Separately, the same page's api-aspect description states that if no webapps are declared for api, its own defaults are mediaweb, hac and authorizationserver. So hac ships by default under backgroundProcessing, backoffice and (as api's own fallback) api - never under admin, which cannot host a web application at all. Attributing hac to the default webapps of the admin aspect is therefore wrong; the correct attribution is backgroundProcessing/backoffice (or api's unconfigured-webapps fallback). | 2211 (CCv2 manifest aspects) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/1be46286b36a4aa48205be5a96240672/8f494fb9617346188ddf21a971db84fc.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-028 |

It is a target-standard transport for quantity-and-date schedule lines, not a custom OCC field. | IEP 2211 / composable storefront 221121.x | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/47ad58c1a27447949aad8addbee46fca/7bcc061e2f3a40b3b34d9e28cc7225db.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-029 |

| For composable storefront 221121.x, SAP documents storefront-label translation resources keyed by language and chunk/namespace, with fallbackLang for missing translations. Local resources may use i18n.backend.loader; a remote HTTPS endpoint uses i18n.backend.loadPath with only {{lng}} and {{ns}} placeholders. SAP documents no built-in BaseSite placeholder or BaseSite-specific translation partition. Therefore standard i18n supports static translations, while preserving distinct per-BaseSite label values requires an explicit per-site resource topology or endpoint-selection design. | Composable storefront 221121.x / SAP Commerce Cloud 2211 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/eaef8c61b6d9477daf75bff9ac1b7eb4/775e61ed219c4999852d43be5244e94a.html | 2026-09-17 | PS-7-032 |

| SAP CONSIDERS THE COMMERCE MIGRATION TOOLKIT (CMT) OBSOLETE AND ARCHIVED, REPLACED BY SAP COMMERCE DB SYNC SINCE 2023-08-09. The GitHub repository README (SAP-archive/commerce-migration-toolkit, redirected from SAP-samples/commerce-migration-toolkit) states verbatim: 'This content is obsolete. CMT has been replaced with SAP Commerce DB Sync tool as of 9th Aug 2023 and this repository should be considered as an archive only. Please refer to the new repository for more details: https://github.com/SAP/sap-commerce-db-sync.' The repo is licensed 'Copyright (c) 2021 SAP SE or an SAP affiliate company... under the Apache Software License, version 2.0', confirming it is an SAP-authored open-source tool, not a customer's own development. The successor repository's own README describes SAP Commerce DB Sync as performing 'table-to-table replication in a single-directional manner between two SAP Commerce instances (onPrem to Cloud) or between SAP Commerce and an external database' for two use cases (analytics replication; one-shot on-prem-to-cloud data migration) - i.e. the successor tool is the SAME architectural class (DB/table-level copy, not business-object-level transformation) as the extension it replaces. Consequence: (1) retiring commercemigration/commercemigrationhac is supported by SAP's own EOL statement; (2) a requirement needing business-object-level data migration is not met by CMT NOR by its documented successor, because both operate at the table/row level - the gap is structural to this tool class, not specific to the deprecated version. | SAP Commerce Migration Toolkit (CMT) / SAP Commerce DB Sync, checked 2026-09-18 | https://github.com/SAP-archive/commerce-migration-toolkit and https://github.com/SAP/sap-commerce-db-sync | 2026-09-18 | PS-7-036 |
| ABSTRACTJOBPERFORMABLE IS A SINGLETON SPRING BEAN REUSED ACROSS CRONJOB RUNS, AND SAP DOCUMENTS A DEDICATED METHOD TO CHECK-AND-RESET THE ABORT FLAG, clearAbortRequestedIfNeeded(cronJob). The 2211 tutorial 'Writing an Abortable Job' wires a job performable as a Spring bean (parent="abstractJobPerformable") with only injected service properties (e.g. l10nService) as fields, and perform(cronJob) receives the CronJobModel as a PARAMETER rather than reading it from instance state - confirming the performable is a stateless, shared bean, not one instance per execution. The documented way to check and clear the abort request is to call the AbstractJobPerformable method clearAbortRequestedIfNeeded(cronJob): 'it checks if the given cronJob is requested to be aborted and has REQUESTABORT flag set to true. If so, the flag is set to false again.' A performable that keeps per-run state in its own fields, or resets the abort flag by hand instead of calling this method, deviates from the documented pattern. | 2211 (AbstractJobPerformable / cronjob tutorial, all 2211-jdk21 releases) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8be670d686691014bdbfac79c33da20e.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-18 | PS-7-037 |
| CLOUD HOT FOLDERS (cloudhotfolder/azurecloudhotfolder) ARE A CSV-TO-IMPEX CONVERTER PIPELINE ON AZURE BLOB STORAGE, NOT A DROP-IN REPLACEMENT FOR RAW FILE PROCESSING - MIGRATING A CUSTOM FILE-DROP JOB ONTO THEM MEANS WRITING A CONVERTER, NOT JUST REPOINTING THE STORAGE. The 2211 'Cloud Hot Folder Extension' page states verbatim: 'Cloud Hot Folders define a customizable pipeline - or flow - that converts CSV files into fast, multithreaded SAP ImpEx scripts. These ImpEx scripts insert or update data directly in your SAP Commerce Cloud database.' It also states 'Hot folders also include a library of converters that allows you to import complex data models, such as product media. If your data model requires complex transformations, you may need to create extra converters.' Consequence: repointing a custom file-drop job to Azure Blob Storage skips the actual migration unit of work - its custom business logic has to become a Hot Folder converter inside a flow/context, and 'Cloud Hot Folders are NOT ... a one-size-fits-all feature; certain use cases may need the flow and behavior ... to be customized' (same page, verbatim). | 2211 (Cloud Hot Folder Extension, CCv2) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/403d43bf9c564f5a985913d1fbfbf8d7/ba9c1917d4234292ba87dc3f9f1acd83.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-18 | PS-7-038 |
| CONTENT CATALOGS HAVE TWO VERSIONS - STAGED (DRAFT) AND ONLINE (CUSTOMER-VISIBLE) - AND ONLY AN EXPLICIT SYNCHRONIZATION PUSHES A CHANGE FROM ONE TO THE OTHER. The 2211 'Synchronizing Content Catalogs' page states verbatim: 'SmartEdit supports two versions of a catalog by default: a staged version and an online version. A staged catalog version is the version that you work with to edit and update the content of your catalog; it is the draft version. The online catalog version contains the published content. This is the version that is displayed to your customers. You push your changes from the staged catalog to the online catalog. This is known as synchronization.' Synchronization is triggered manually/by job (a Sync button per catalog version, or the equivalent cron/console action) and copies components, content slots, shared content slots, navigation nodes, pages, page templates and restrictions - it is not automatic or continuous. Consequence for reading any ImpEx that wires a CMS component into a Staged catalog version: this is, by itself, evidence of an editorial/preview-time change only; whether it is customer-visible depends on whether a synchronization to the Online catalog version has run (or will run) in that environment, which the ImpEx alone does not establish - the repository fact is 'placed in Staged', not 'live on the storefront'. | 2211 (SmartEdit / Content Catalog staged-online synchronization, all SAP Commerce Cloud versions) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/8d5b806fe6a44da681c9df6edc6f6971/59e90f11556b4567aa5282337ae73782.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-18 | PS-7-039 |

Consequence: a project extension that declares exactly one Spring bean of this class pointing Backoffice's label resolution at its own <extension>-backoffice-labels/ folder is using the SAP-documented mechanism unmodified, not a custom label-lookup implementation - the customization is limited to the folder path and bean id, never the mechanism. | 2211-jdk21 (Backoffice UI localization, com.hybris.cockpitng.util.labels.ResourcesLabelLocator) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/9b5366ff6eb34df5be29881ff55f97d2/8c06859f8669101492e5c1d7d5724e8e.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-18 | PS-7-041 |
| THE DEP-COCKPITS SCANNER PATTERN'S SEVEN NAMED IDENTIFIERS ARE NOT UNIFORMLY 'REMOVED LEGACY COCKPITS' AGAINST THE VERIFIED 2211 RECORD - THE PATTERN'S OWN SEED IS FLAGGED UNVERIFIED AND ITS DATE IS WRONG FOR THE PART THAT IS REAL. The engine's knowledge pack defines DEP-COCKPITS as the regex cmscockpit/productcockpit/cscockpit/ordercockpit/reportcockpit/importcockpit/printcockpit/\bcockpit\b with seed metadata 'since: Removed in 2205 (deprecated since 1808)' and 'verify: true' (i.e. flagged as not yet checked against a dated release note). PS-7-014 in this section (researched from the actual 2211 What's New / Deprecated Cockpit Extensions pages) found a different, verified fact: the real, dated removal is exactly EIGHT extensions - cockpit, admincockpit, cmscockpit, productcockpit, configurablebundlecockpits, subscriptioncockpits, subscriptionbundlecockpits, yacceleratorcockpits - removed in 2211-jdk21.1 (valid as of 2025-09-09), NOT 2205, and PS-7-014 explicitly states 'cscockpit and reportcockpit are NOT on this list and are NOT rows in the 2211 Deprecation Status table either'. Consequence for any scope whose analysis explains a DEP-COCKPITS hit by asserting the pattern names 'seven removed legacy cockpits': only two of the seven named identifiers (cmscockpit, productcockpit) correspond to a verified, dated SAP removal (2211-jdk21.1, per PS-7-014) - two more (cscockpit, reportcockpit) are documented by PS-7-014 as carrying NO removal or deprecation-table entry at all, and the remaining three (ordercockpit, importcockpit, printcockpit) are addressed by neither PS-7-014 nor any other row in this section. A false-positive verdict on a DEP-COCKPITS hit (the hit string names none of the pattern's seven identifiers, only the generic \bcockpit\b catch-all) remains sound regardless, but the phrase 'removed legacy cockpits' applied uniformly to all seven overstates what is actually documented and should either cite PS-7-014 by id and restrict the removal claim to cmscockpit/productcockpit, or drop the removal framing for the pattern as a whole. | SAP Commerce Cloud 2211 (DEP-COCKPITS heuristic vs. verified cockpit-removal record) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/58a4ff4e9d304a04a5ae809a024da902.html | 2026-09-18 | PS-7-042 |

| THE PLATFORM XSS FILTER'S PROPERTIES ARE DOCUMENTED AS EXTENSION-PREFIXED AND ATTACH PER WEB APPLICATION, NOT GLOBALLY BY DEFAULT: the Secure Configuration page documents de.hybris.platform.servicelayer.web.XSSFilter, configured via global properties (xss.filter.enabled, xss.filter.action, xss.filter.rule.<id>) that 'you can also disable/enable... per extension by adding the extension name as a prefix to the parameter' (worked example hac.xss.filter.enabled=false, hac.xss.filter.action=REJECT); the filter is registered in a web application's own web.xml (worked example: filter-class XSSFilter mapped to /* in that web app). | THE INTEGRATION API PATCH ENDPOINT IS A DOCUMENTED, DELIVERED COLLECTION-REPLACE MECHANISM: A SHORTER COLLECTION IN THE PATCH BODY DELETES THE REMAINDER. The 'The PATCH API' page (Integrations and Data Management, 2211) states verbatim: every Integration Object has a PATCH endpoint that updates only the attributes present in the payload (omitted attributes are unchanged) - so PATCH, not POST, is the delivered partial-update operation. Its PATCH API Constraints section states verbatim: 'If a PATCH request includes fewer values than currently exist for an attribute, the service deletes the existing values and persists only the values provided in the PATCH request body' (worked example: a customer with three email addresses PATCHed with one address ends up with only that one) - i.e. PATCHing a shorter collection IS the documented mechanism for collection replacement, including deletion of unlisted members. Also documented: 'You can set collections to an empty collection, but you cannot set a collection to null'; PATCH requires integrationadmingroup or integrationcreategroup membership, updates exactly one Integration Object per request, and does not support query filters. Consequence: a custom delete-and-replace POST hook for a whole sub-collection reproduces, with bespoke code, exactly the semantics the standard PATCH endpoint already delivers for that same Integration Object. | SAP Commerce Cloud 2211 / Integration API (odata2webservices PATCH) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/25aa5bc96b3e4ceb958a4aee039db7c1.html | 2026-09-21 | PS-7-053 |
| THE PERSISTENCE-HOOKS PAGE DOCUMENTS BOTH AN EXCEPTION-HANDLING AND A TRANSACTION-MANAGEMENT CONTRACT FOR PrePersistHook/PostPersistHook, VERBATIM. Exceptions: 'If an exception is thrown during the execution of the PrePersistHook, the exception is logged, and the integration item included in the POST request does not persist. An error response code, pre_persist_error, and the status code 400 return' - i.e. an exception that ESCAPES the hook method aborts persistence; this is the framework's own safety net, distinct from a hook that catches an exception internally and returns normally (Optional.of(item)), which persists the item as usual because no exception ever reaches the framework. Transactions: 'A new transaction begins before execution of the PrePersistHook. The transaction commits after the PostPersistHook completes ... If you start a new transaction within a PrePersistHook or a PostPersistHook ... your transaction might not roll back, which could lead to an inconsistent state. The same is true for starting a new thread ... For this reason, do not start a new database transaction or thread within a persistence hook.' | SAP Commerce Cloud 2211 / Integration API (inboundservices Persistence Hooks: exception handling and transaction management) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/bad9b0b66bac476f8a4a5c4a08e4ab6b/028a2af06880407cb4b1c0624693dadd.html | 2026-09-21 | PS-7-054 |

| INTEGRATION OBJECT AUTHENTICATION VIA InboundChannelConfiguration WITH BASIC AUTHENTICATION IS A DOCUMENTED, STANDARD INTEGRATION API MECHANISM, NOT A CUSTOM BUILD. The 2211 'Integration Object Authentication' page states verbatim: 'In Backoffice, you can authenticate an Integration Object with OAuth or Basic authentication. Exposing Integration Objects as APIs with enhanced authentication requires an InboundChannelConfiguration, which defines the type of authentication used for API requests. Three CCv2 pages together establish this. (1) 'Blob Storage' (Cloud Portal): CCv2 Blob storage allocations are exactly three named types - hot folders (file import), logs (application logs) and 'media storage', which 'SAP Commerce Cloud uses ... for things like storefront imagery, video, and texts', with content 'made available through standard Commerce Cloud services, APIs, and media URLs as appropriate' - i.e. the platform's own Media abstraction, not a project's direct Azure SDK calls, is what lands in this allocation. (2) 'Managing Media Storage Accounts' (Cloud Extensions) confirms this storage is populated by an out-of-the-box 'media storage strategy' with its own standard housekeeping (the mediadeletions table, purgeDeletedFilesCronJob). (3) 'Platform Module Features' (Platform, Services, and Utilities) names 'manage media storage strategies' as a general Platform-module capability, not one scoped to product images. Media (de.hybris.platform.core.model.media.MediaModel) and MediaService are platform-generic: any extension may relate its own item types to Media, write to it through MediaService, and get CCv2 Blob storage for free without touching the Azure SDK - this is a materially different, higher-level and already-standard path from the cloudhotfolder chain (which is documented only as an inbound CSV-to-ImpEx import mechanism, PS-7-038, and never claims to cover outbound business-triggered writes). | SAP COMMERCE 2211 SHIPS AND ACTIVELY MAINTAINS THE LOG4J 1.x COMPATIBILITY BRIDGE log4j-1.2-api, AND THE 2211 DEPRECATION STATUS TABLE CARRIES NO LOG4J ROW AT ALL. Method and result. (a) Full-text case-insensitive search of the rendered 2211 Deprecation Status table (the same page and method as PS-7-015, PS-7-068, PS-7-071) for the strings log4j, log4, logging and slf4j returns ZERO matches for each. (b) The JDK-21-line release notes list org.apache.logging.log4j:log4j-1.2-api under Upgraded Libraries twice and never under Deleted Libraries: 2.24.1 -> 2.25.3 at Update Release 2211-jdk21.9 (https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/75d4c3895cb346008545900bffe851ce/08f5977d5faa46d28ccc0f783ebb8e58.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211) and 2.25.3 -> 2.25.4 at 2211-jdk21.14 (.../1470e56651ac4ea886aa9c5dffb5b575.html). That artifact is the Apache bridge that provides the org.apache.log4j package on top of Log4j 2: apache/logging-log4j2 branch 2.x, log4j-1.2-api/src/main/java/org/apache/log4j/Logger.java declares `package org.apache.log4j;` and imports org.apache.logging.log4j.spi.LoggerContext. (c) SAP's own position page, Logging with Log4j 2 Through SLF4J (Platform, Services, and Utilities, 2211, .../aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8b2c4a4286691014894a812a097cb276.html), says SAP Commerce Cloud comes prebundled with Apache Log4j 2, that Log4j 2 is the standard logging framework, and that SLF4J is RECOMMENDED as the logging API - a recommendation, not a deprecation. (d) The only log4j-1 item SAP itself calls deprecated is the CONFIGURATION PROPERTY syntax: Upgrading to Log4j 2 (2211, .../b4b7ed2ba3ce40ef8f9d93e95eefba42.html) documents log4j.deprecated.properties.warn=true (default) and log4j.deprecated.properties.print=false to detect residual Log4j 1.x properties, and asks that they be replaced by their log4j2.* equivalents. CONSEQUENCE for a migration verdict: custom Java code importing org.apache.log4j.* compiles and runs at 2211 through a vendor-shipped, vendor-patched bridge - it is a code-modernisation item (SLF4J is the recommended API), not a dated removal and not a migration blocker. Keep three different things apart: log4j-1 API usage in Java code (bridged, works), log4j-1 CONFIGURATION properties (SAP-flagged deprecated, warns at startup), and custom log4j2 XML configuration, which Configuring Your Log Environment (2211, .../0e16e56bae534d0ca6ed807158433209.html) states is NOT supported on SAP Commerce Cloud (do not use log4j2.config.xml; custom appenders are unsupported and may cause server restarts). | 2211 / 2211-jdk21 (Deprecation Status table; Library Changes of Update Releases 2211-jdk21.9 and 2211-jdk21.14; Platform logging documentation) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/12be4ac419604b01aabb1adeb2c4c8a2/8bb15ed586691014a948d1553f4947cf.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-22 | PS-7-074 |

(a) Shipped version: the 2211-jdk21 Library Changes list org.springframework.security:spring-security-{core,config,web,test,ldap,crypto,taglibs,oauth2-*,saml2-service-provider} at 6.5.5 -> 6.5.8 / 6.5.10 (Update Release 2211-jdk21.14) and 6.5.10 -> 6.5.11 (2211-jdk21.15 and 2211-jdk21.18). (b) SAP position: the 2211 Spring Security topic (Commerce deliverable, https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/e1391e5265574bfbb56ca4c0573ba1dc/45d6a34878964c6b9485a769a79c62cb.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211) gives the reference web-service security configuration ENTIRELY in the XML namespace - <security:http use-authorization-manager="true" use-expressions="true">, <security:intercept-url>, <security:port-mappings>, <security:custom-filter>, <security:access-denied-handler>, <security:oauth2-resource-server>, <security:headers>, <security:csrf> in web/webroot/WEB-INF/config/security-spring.xml, wired through springSecurityFilterChain inside the PlatformFilterChain. SAP documents NO Java/Lambda DSL alternative for Commerce extensions. (c) SAP's own Spring 6 migration task list, Spring Framework 6.2.x (Update Releases 2211, .../75d4c3895cb346008545900bffe851ce/4f86494bd4574ae4b5d29f7dc9b8e5b6.html), names ~24 manual tasks, and its three Spring-Security tasks all tell customers to EDIT the XML rather than leave it: (1) Updating Spring Security Intercept-URL Configuration for Empty Expression Strings (.../f5f321a8260e43618fd151913baab658.html) - in Spring Security 6 every <security:intercept-url> must carry an explicit access attribute; a rule with only requires-channel fails startup with "expressionString cannot be empty"; the documented fix is adding access="permitAll()" in spring-security-config.xml; (2) Fixing "No visible WebSecurityExpressionHandler" Error (.../5fe2a1a6280d4c8c803e38509a50c629.html) - missing security expression handler for JSP authorize tags, IOException at startup; (3) Resolving Redirection Issues of request-matcher (.../f53b87feec67434999c6d7a6b846e731.html) - the Spring Security 6 default matcher changed from AntPathRequestMatcher to MvcPathRequestMatcher, producing 302 redirects on static resources. (d) Upstream: Spring Security 6.5.x still documents Namespace Configuration as a first-class option next to Java and Kotlin configuration (spring-projects/spring-security, branch 6.5.x, docs/modules/ROOT/nav.adoc lines 113-115 and docs/modules/ROOT/pages/servlet/configuration/xml-namespace.adoc); a full-text search of that 406-line page for "deprecat" returns ZERO matches. The Spring item actually REMOVED at Spring Security 6.0 is WebSecurityConfigurerAdapter, a Java-config class, not the XML namespace. CONSEQUENCE: a custom extension declaring the Spring Security XML namespace carries NO deprecation, vendor or upstream - do not file it as one. (b) All 37 change pages of the 2211-jdk21 release notes were downloaded and searched as a corpus - Library Changes, Deprecation Status Changes, API/Data Model/Spring Framework Changes and Third-Party Compatibility for Update Releases 2211-jdk21.1, .2, .4, .5, .6, .7, .9, .10, .11, .13, .14, .15, .17 and .18 - and the strings jaxws, jax-ws, com.sun.xml.ws and metro return ZERO matches.
