Conclusion: SAP Commerce 2211 on SapMachine 21 (required JDK per Third-Party Compatibility at 2211-jdk21.17, which also bundles Tomcat 10.1.57) does NOT bundle a JAX-WS runtime and has published no supported / deprecated / removed statement about one. (c) What SAP DOES document as its own outbound mechanisms is REST- and destination-oriented with no SOAP-client equivalent named: Endpoints and Destinations (Platform, Services, and Utilities 2211, https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/af5e0116579a4e1b893089f9f956e47d.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211) with exposed and consumed destinations plus connection test, the Outbound Sync / outbound service path (Integrations and Data Management 2211), and ApiRegistryRestClient as the documented registry-based client class. (d) Bundling one's own runtime is nevertheless an explicitly supported PACKAGING mechanism, which is the distinction that matters for a verdict: Extension Library Management Using Maven (2211, .../aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/120f6d7b89a745018cb28b5e34318fa4.html) documents usemaven="true" in extensioninfo.xml together with external-dependencies.xml, and states that libraries under <extension>/lib "will be added to the global class path of Platform (which is why we need to align with other extensions shipping the same)". CONSEQUENCE: report a self-bundled com.sun.xml.ws:jaxws-rt, Metro call parameters or a jaxws-maven-plugin as a VENDOR-UNSUPPORTED, self-maintained SOAP runtime sitting on the platform's global classpath - the project owns its version conflicts and CVE exposure, and SAP names no standard SOAP-client route to migrate to. Do NOT file it as a vendor deprecation; the accurate client wording is "SAP documents no position". | 2211 / 2211-jdk21 | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/120f6d7b89a745018cb28b5e34318fa4.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-22 | PS-7-077 |
| ObjectUtils.equals IS DEPRECATED BUT STILL PRESENT IN THE commons-lang3 VERSION 2211 SHIPS; THE REAL REMOVAL ON THE SAME PAGE IS commons-lang 2.x, DELETED AT 2211-jdk21.1; JAVA assert HAS NO VENDOR POSITION. (a) Shipped version: the Library Changes of Update Release 2211-jdk21.1 (https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/75d4c3895cb346008545900bffe851ce/71fee77e332e49b0a3cdefac6dc3ddb8.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211) upgrade org.apache.commons:commons-lang3 from 3.9 to 3.17.0 for Platform, with 3.17.0 -> 3.18.0 entries for other modules on the same page. (b) In BOTH 3.17.0 and 3.18.0 the method still exists and is merely annotated @Deprecated - it is NOT removed: apache/commons-lang, tags rel/commons-lang-3.17.0 and rel/commons-lang-3.18.0, src/main/java/org/apache/commons/lang3/ObjectUtils.java, javadoc "this method has been replaced by java.util.Objects.equals(Object, Object) in Java 7 and will be removed from future releases", body `return Objects.equals(object1, object2);`. So the call compiles and behaves identically at 2211; the remedy is a one-line replacement with java.util.Objects.equals. This is a code-quality / technical-debt item, not a migration blocker and not an SAP deprecation - SAP itself states no position on it (see the negative in (d)). (c) The genuine hard break a scanner can easily confuse with it, found on the SAME page: commons-lang:commons-lang - that is commons-lang 2.x, Java package org.apache.commons.lang WITHOUT the 3 - appears in the DELETED LIBRARIES list of Update Release 2211-jdk21.1. Any import of org.apache.commons.lang.* therefore does not compile at 2211-jdk21 unless the extension bundles the JAR itself. Always check which package a hit sits in before classifying it: org.apache.commons.lang3.ObjectUtils.equals = deprecated-but-works; org.apache.commons.lang.* = removed. (d) The assert keyword used as input validation is a Java-language question with NO vendor position: Java Development Guidelines for SAP Commerce Cloud (Development Guidelines 2211, .../51e73d14aedc487384e4518b60a1f5fd/2e2d35a17d0e4ab8a0282d660d2531c1.html) delegates Java coding rules to "the SAP Commerce Cloud SonarQube quality profile that ships with the SAP Commerce Cloud product" in the build-tools/sonarqube directory, and states explicitly that the rules in that profile "are not repeated in this section"; a full read of that page finds no mention of assertions, preconditions or input validation. CONSEQUENCE: file assert-as-input-validation as a code-quality observation measurable against the project's own shipped Sonar profile, never as a deprecation, and say plainly that SAP publishes no position on it. | 2211 / 2211-jdk21 (commons-lang3 3.17.0/3.18.0 and the deletion of commons-lang 2.x at 2211-jdk21.1; Java Development Guidelines) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/75d4c3895cb346008545900bffe851ce/71fee77e332e49b0a3cdefac6dc3ddb8.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-22 | PS-7-078 |
| THE DOCUMENTED 2211 PASSWORD-ENCODER CONTRACT IS THE PROPERTY default.password.encoding WITH ARGON2 AS DEFAULT AND EXACTLY THREE SHIPPED STRATEGIES; ALL LEGACY ENCODERS AND legacy.password.encoding.enabled WERE DELETED AT 2211-jdk21.5 - A REMOVAL, NOT A DEPRECATION. (a) Two 2211 pages state the contract identically: Password Encoding (SAP Commerce Cloud Security Guide 2211, https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/ddc1ef9fc7fa48cb90f2b57c061a4012/cdf5af8d1fdf428ca10df6084d4a60b6.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211) and Password Storage Strategies (Platform, Services, and Utilities 2211, .../aa417173fe4a4ba5a473c93eb730a417/8c256c54866910149de4d53a72e973d4.html). The property is default.password.encoding - NOT any token spelled "DEFAULT-PASSWORD-ENCODER" - "the default strategy in SAP Commerce Cloud is Argon2", and the shipped secure strategies are exactly three: argon2 (strongest, OWASP-recommended), scrypt (recommended if Argon2 is impossible), bcrypt (strong, kept for backward compatibility), resolved through the core.passwordEncoderFactory bean in ext/core/resources/core-spring.xml. Custom encoders remain a documented extension point, with the caution to preserve the built-in entries. (b) Password Storage Strategies additionally documents password.encoding.auto.update.enabled (true by default): on login, a password hashed with a non-default algorithm - or with the default algorithm but older parameters - is rehashed with the default; the configurable Argon2 parameters are password.encoder.argon2.memory (default 19456), .parallelism (1), .iterations (2), .saltLength (16), .hashLength (32). Auto-update works only for argon2, bcrypt and scrypt. (c) Deletion of Deprecated Password Hashing Algorithms (What's New in SAP Commerce Cloud in the Public Cloud 2211, .../251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/c00fa1fc70fb44bf94594340ce3022a5.html) records the removal as IMPLEMENTED at Version 2211-jdk21.5, valid as of 2025-12-09, first announced at 2211-jdk21.3 on 2025-11-04, Adopted By 2026-06-09: the legacy.password.encoding.enabled flag is removed and these classes are eliminated - MD5PasswordEncoder, SaltedMD5PasswordEncoder, PKbasedSaltedMD5PasswordEncoder, SHA1PasswordEncoder, PlainTextPasswordEncoder, PBKDF2WithHmacSHA1SaltedPasswordEncoder, DeprecatedPasswordEncoder, GenericSaltedPasswordEncoder, SaltedPasswordEncoder. Users whose passwords were hashed with a removed algorithm and never re-encoded can no longer log in and must reset their password; Argon2 remains the default and bcrypt a secure alternative. This CONFIRMS the seed row that named 2211-jdk21.5. (d) A confirmed negative on the deprecation table: a full-text case-insensitive search of the rendered 2211 Deprecation Status table for encoder, encoding and argon returns ZERO matches, and all four password matches are deprecated OCC ENDPOINTS, not encoders - POST /{baseSiteId}/forgottenpasswordtokens and PUT /{baseSiteId}/users/{userId}/password, both announced 2211.24 with planned deletion "To be determined", replaced by POST /{baseSiteId}/passwordRestoreToken and POST /{baseSiteId}/users/{userId}/password. (e) No CCv2-SPECIFIC REQUIRED encoder is documented: the documentation states a platform default plus a recommendation ("SAP recommends that you use a secure encoding strategy from an early stage"), and still documents ImpEx setting an explicit encoding via $passwordEncoding=bcrypt with ConvertPlaintextToEncodedUserPasswordTranslator. Anything stronger than "Argon2 is the documented default and the legacy encoders no longer exist" would be general practice, not documented vendor requirement. CONSEQUENCE: a repository hit naming a legacy encoder is a HARD BREAK from 2211-jdk21.5 onwards - class gone, flag gone, affected users locked out until a password reset. But a hit that is only an ImpEx MACRO DEFINITION (for example $passwordEncoding=md5 in a header macro) is still only a definition, per the existing seed rule. | 2211 / 2211-jdk21.5 (Password Encoding security guide; Password Storage Strategies; Deletion of Deprecated Password Hashing Algorithms; Deprecation Status table negative) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_CLOUD_PUBLIC_CLOUD/251a1313585a498eb998c2f974f4f338/c00fa1fc70fb44bf94594340ce3022a5.html?locale=en-US&state=PRODUCTION&version=v2211 | 2026-09-22 | PS-7-079 |

The 2211 Cloud Portal page 'Commerce Logs Blob Storage' states verbatim: SAP Commerce Cloud provides a dedicated Azure Blob storage for your logs; logs appear in a container named commerce-logs-separated grouped by pod with one file per day; the system compresses the logs in GZ format when it sends them to Azure Blob storage; retention is 30 days (dev/stg) and 90 days (production), after which blobs are deleted automatically. The 2211 page 'Configuring Expired Session Cleanup for Clustered Environments' documents configurable properties for discovering and purging expired sessions from persistence in clustered environments: default.session.timeout (default 3600s, session validity baseline), session.discovery.cron.expression (default '0 0/5 * ? * * *', every 5 minutes - the schedule the discovery job would use once created) and session.discovery.batch.size (default 1000). Consequence for reading a repository that ships the discovery bean/strategy class (platformservices) but no ImpEx creating an expiredSessionDiscovery CronJob instance: this is exactly the documented, expected state before an operator acts - the platform supplies the mechanism and its tuning properties, but the CronJob itself is never auto-created, so its absence is not evidence of a missing standard capability, only of an unfinished configuration step. Default is false.' On a standalone <collectiontype>, the type attribute (values set/list/collection) 'Configures the type of this collection: set, list, collection ... | SAP COMMERCE'S OWN SESSION CONCEPT (JaloSession, WHICH SessionService's LOCAL-VIEW API OPERATES ON) IS DOCUMENTED AS AN IN-MEMORY, PER-THREAD USER-CONTEXT OBJECT, ORTHOGONAL TO DATABASE TRANSACTIONS AND THREADS. The 'Jalo Session' page states verbatim: 'The JaloSession is a core concept of SAP Commerce Cloud. A JaloSession wraps up data about the current user and their settings. It forms a context in which SAP Commerce Cloud processes user-specific requests', 'There's always 1 JaloSession active for a given thread' (accessed via the static JaloSession.getCurrentSession() on the CALLING thread, not a new one), and 'JaloSession objects are held in memory only, they aren't made persistent (written into the database)' - i.e. creating, entering or leaving a (local) session changes which user/language/currency context the CURRENT thread sees; it neither starts a new thread nor opens or closes a database transaction, which are Spring/ServiceLayer-managed constructs entirely separate from the session object. Consequence: a job/service bean that keeps per-run state as instance fields (e.g. the running CronJob, its model, or a running log/message buffer) written and read across the perform() call is exposed to exactly the concurrency hazard the vendor interface itself flags whenever two CronJob records reference the same bean id and a run overlaps (manual trigger while scheduled trigger runs, or two records naming the same job) - this is a documented framework contract violation, not merely a project code-style nit. | 2211-jdk21 (servicelayer CronJob framework: JobPerformable / AbstractJobPerformable / ServicelayerJob) | https://help.sap.com/doc/789b8a6e1434444da824c2493c6faaa0/2211.4/en-US/de/hybris/platform/servicelayer/cronjob/JobPerformable.html | 2026-09-23 | PS-7-093 |

| THE BACKOFFICE CONTEXT parent/merge-by ATTRIBUTES ARE THE DOCUMENTED MECHANISM FOR LINKING A NEW CONTEXT TO AN EXISTING ONE FOR ADDITIVE MERGING; WITHOUT THEM A CONTEXT'S CONTENT DOES NOT INHERIT FROM A RELATED CONTEXT, AND THE IDENTICAL-KEY COLLISION CASE IS UNSETTLED BY THIS PAGE. The 2211 'Context Elements in the Backoffice Configuration' page documents the context tag's 'parent' attribute ('It specifies a parent configuration which is supposed to be merged', default 'auto') and 'merge-by' attribute ('Together with the parent attribute, it declares by which properties the configuration should be merged'), worked through a type='Workflow' context declaring merge-by='type' parent='CronJob' to add a CronJob-defined simple-search field to Workflow's own field, closing verbatim with: 'If the merge-by was not used, the Workflow type would be searchable only by field name.' This settles that a context without merge-by/parent does not pull in a related context's fields - it is resolved on its own declared content. It does NOT settle the narrower case a custom backoffice extension can raise: two context blocks sharing the IDENTICAL type+component signature (e.g. type='Item' component='advanced-search', or type='<CustomItemType>' component='create-wizard') declared in two different extensions with neither block carrying merge-by/parent at all - whether the platform's build-time XML merge (PS-7-056) treats an identical-key pair as one auto-unioned node (via the parent='auto' default) or as a plain override where the later-loaded extension's block is the one resolved is not stated on this page or in the worked example, which only covers a declared parent-child link between two DIFFERENT type keys. Separately, de.hybris.platform.servicelayer.exceptions.ModelSavingException extends SystemException, and SystemException extends java.lang.RuntimeException (both confirmed from their 2211.4 javadoc class-hierarchy headers) - i.e. ModelSavingException is an unchecked exception under Java language rules, so a method is never required to declare it in a throws clause or a javadoc @throws tag even where an internal code path (e.g. a checkState(!isNew)-style guard before persisting) can throw it. CONSEQUENCE: a claim that the shipped DefaultMediaService implementation of setStreamForMedia throws an uncaught ModelSavingException from an internal state-check before persisting is not contradicted by the interface's documented throws clause (MediaIOException/IllegalArgumentException only covers the CHECKED exceptions of the public contract); confirming the specific internal code path itself requires repository/bytecode evidence outside vendor documentation. | 2211-jdk21 (servicelayer: de.hybris.platform.servicelayer.media.MediaService.setStreamForMedia; de.hybris.platform.servicelayer.exceptions.ModelSavingException / SystemException) | https://help.sap.com/doc/789b8a6e1434444da824c2493c6faaa0/2211.4/en-US/de/hybris/platform/servicelayer/media/MediaService.html and https://help.sap.com/doc/789b8a6e1434444da824c2493c6faaa0/2211.4/en-US/de/hybris/platform/servicelayer/exceptions/ModelSavingException.html and https://help.sap.com/doc/789b8a6e1434444da824c2493c6faaa0/2211.4/en-US/de/hybris/platform/servicelayer/exceptions/SystemException.html | 2026-09-23 | PS-7-096 |
| THE 2211 TYPE PERMISSIONS DOC STATES VERBATIM THAT A PRINCIPAL WITH TYPE-LEVEL PERMISSION HAS ACCESS TO ALL ATTRIBUTES OF THAT TYPE UNLESS A MORE SPECIFIC ATTRIBUTE PERMISSION IS ASSIGNED. The 2211 'Type Permissions' page (Commerce, SmartEdit/permissionswebservices backend) states verbatim: 'Principals with permissions to a type also have access to all the attributes defined of the type, unless more specific attribute permissions are assigned.' It also documents a Type Permission Hierarchy (child types inherit and may override the parent type's rights) and a priority rule ('SmartEdit adheres to the access rights assigned to an attribute over the rights assigned for an item' - example: READ granted on the whole ContentPage type but denied on its Name attribute leaves every attribute readable except Name). The underlying Permission Services framework (Managing and Checking Access Rights, 2211) confirms the same layering generically: permissions can be assigned to items, types AND attribute descriptors, with type assignments inherited down the type hierarchy and attribute assignments as the most specific, highest-priority override; absent any attribute-level assignment the type-level (or its inherited) permission is what applies. | THE @SystemSetup PATCHING FRAMEWORK RUNS ONCE-ONLY LOGIC DURING SYSTEM INITIALIZATION/UPDATE AND TRACKS EVERY APPLIED PATCH IN A DATABASE-PERSISTED SystemSetupAudit ITEM, READABLE ONLY AT RUNTIME, NEVER FROM THE REPOSITORY. The 2211 'Patching Framework' page documents verbatim: 'You can annotate certain Spring beans with @SystemSetup. Logic annotated in this way is then executed during system initialization or update. | SPRING SECURITY XML NAMESPACE <authentication-manager> WITHOUT AN id ATTRIBUTE ALWAYS RESOLVES TO THE FIXED BEAN NAME org.springframework.security.authenticationManager AND OVERWRITES ANY EXISTING BEAN OF THAT NAME WITH ONLY A LOG WARNING. AuthenticationManagerBeanDefinitionParser.parse() (6.5.11 source): 'String id = element.getAttribute("id"); if (!StringUtils.hasText(id)) { if (pc.getRegistry().containsBeanDefinition(BeanIds.AUTHENTICATION_MANAGER)) { pc.getReaderContext().warning("Overriding globally registered AuthenticationManager", ...); } id = BeanIds.AUTHENTICATION_MANAGER; }' followed by 'pc.registerBeanComponent(new BeanComponentDefinition(providerManagerBldr.getBeanDefinition(), id))' - Spring's bean registry registers (and by default overwrites) a bean definition under that id. BeanIds.AUTHENTICATION_MANAGER is the constant 'org.springframework.security.authenticationManager' (PREFIX + "authenticationManager"). CONSEQUENCE: when two Spring XML files in the same context each declare an <authentication-manager> block with no id, whichever is parsed LAST wins the shared bean name (with only a log warning, no startup failure), and any earlier <authentication-provider>/success-handler wiring nested inside that block is replaced along with it - independent of, and not gated by, which HTTP security filter chain (<http> block) is actually selected to serve a request. | Spring Security 6.5.x XML namespace config (authentication-manager element, AuthenticationManagerBeanDefinitionParser, BeanIds), as shipped SAP Commerce Cloud 2211-jdk21 (spring-security-config-6.5.11.jar) | https://github.com/spring-projects/spring-security/raw/6.5.11/config/src/main/java/org/springframework/security/config/authentication/AuthenticationManagerBeanDefinitionParser.java and https://github.com/spring-projects/spring-security/raw/6.5.11/config/src/main/java/org/springframework/security/config/BeanIds.java | 2026-09-23 | PS-7-099 |
| RegexRequestMatcher MATCHES AGAINST servletPath + pathInfo + '?' + queryString WITH A FULL Matcher.matches() TEST, NOT A PATH-ONLY prefix/find() TEST. RegexRequestMatcher (6.5.11 source) javadoc verbatim: 'The match is performed against the servletPath + pathInfo + queryString of the request and is case-sensitive by default.' Its matches(HttpServletRequest) builds url from request.getServletPath(), appends request.getPathInfo() if present and appends '?' + request.getQueryString() if a query string is present, then returns 'this.pattern.matcher(url).matches()' - a full-string match, not Matcher.find(). CONSEQUENCE: a request-matcher class that instead checks only request.getServletPath() (ignoring pathInfo/queryString) can exclude/match a different set of requests than a RegexRequestMatcher-based security intercept-url rule guarding the same path - appending an otherwise-inert query parameter to a request changes what a RegexRequestMatcher sees (and can move a request out of a regex pattern's match) without changing what a servletPath-only matcher sees. | Spring Security 6.5.x web request matchers (RegexRequestMatcher), as shipped SAP Commerce Cloud 2211-jdk21 (spring-security-web-6.5.11.jar) | https://github.com/spring-projects/spring-security/raw/6.5.11/web/src/main/java/org/springframework/security/web/util/matcher/RegexRequestMatcher.java | 2026-09-23 | PS-7-100 |

The 2211 'SAP Digital Payments Add-On Integration Implementation' page lists as prerequisites verbatim 'You must have a valid subscription to SAP digital payments add-on' and tenant ID, client ID and client secret configured on the add-on; the process covers card registration via redirect, authorization at order placement, capture in the order process and refund in the return process. The composable storefront Integrations page describes SAP Digital Payments as 'ready-to-use PSP connectivity' and the SAP Commerce Cloud, open payment framework as 'a SaaS solution for managing your payment integrations'. Consequence: a requirement marked covered by the standard through Digital Payments or OPF depends on an additional SAP subscription plus a PSP contract; if a paid add-on does not count as standard, the Commerce side is a standard integration, the payment service is not. See also the synchronous S/4HANA order creation mode, which excludes card payment (row on S/4HANA Order Management modes in this section). | IEP 2211 (SAP Digital Payments Add-On Integration) / composable storefront (SAP Digital Payments Integration 4.1/2105; OPF) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/4f00a6453e4242bbac5b3cb82b616576/bc5fa9a6ad4a467c8facda6895d24644.html?version=2211 ; https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_COMPOSABLE_STOREFRONT/962112809f9a48f9b36aa05b208b3731/1c59d38bd56941aeb15c8333dfa0d780.html | 2026-09-24 | PS-7-112 |
| SAP ENTERPRISE PRODUCT DEVELOPMENT (EPD) VISUALIZATION - THE ONLY DOCUMENTED COMPOSABLE-STOREFRONT INTEGRATION FOR 2D/3D VISUAL SPARE-PART PICKING - IS DEPRECATED AND NEEDS ITS OWN SUBSCRIPTION. The composable storefront integration page states verbatim: 'Starting with release 221121.5 of composable storefront, SAP Enterprise Product Development Visualization Integration is deprecated', and under Requirements 'requires release 2105 of SAP Commerce Cloud, as well as a SAP Enterprise Product Development subscription'. The Feature Compatibility Matrix row reads 'DEPRECATED - SAP Enterprise Product Development Visualization Integration' 4.3 / 2105. | InboundOMMOrder / InboundOMMOrderEntry ARE STATUS-NOTIFICATION OBJECTS FOR ORDERS THAT ORIGINATED IN SAP COMMERCE, NOT A REPLICATION OF ALL SALES DOCUMENTS. The 2211 page 'Replicating Inbound Cart and Checkout Order Notifications from the SAP Back End to SAP Commerce Cloud' (SAP Cloud Integration) states: the flow 'supports notification replications for sales orders based on the Order Management Module (OMM) in SAP Commerce Cloud'; prerequisite extension sapcpiorderexchange (B2C) or sapcpiorderexchangeb2b (B2B); 'The inbound integration objects InboundOMMOrder and InboundOMMOrderEntry are based on the item types Order and OrderEntry'; 'InboundOMMOrderEntry defines the OData POST request for updating the named delivery dates for order entries directly, without triggering a persistence hook'; InboundOMMOrder carries exactly four notifications with their hooks - OMM Order Confirmation (sapCpiOmmOrderConfirmationPersistenceHook), OMM Order Cancellation (sapCpiOmmOrderCancellationPersistenceHook), OMM Order Delivery (sapCpiOmmDeliveryPersistenceHook), OMM Order Goods Issue (sapCpiOmmGoodsIssuePersistenceHook); the iflow is 'Replicate Cart and Checkout Order Notifications from SAP ERP to SAP Commerce Cloud' (process direct /s4hana/OmmOrder). The page documents no creation of orders that were placed outside the shop (phone, e-mail, ERP-direct) and no quotes, invoices, credit memos or handling units. The OMS variant is InboundOMSOrder on SAPOrder (sapcpiorderexchangeoms). Consequence: InboundOMMOrder covers status, cancellation, delivery and goods issue of shop-originated orders; a channel-independent read copy of all sales document types is not covered and stays custom. | SAP Commerce Cloud 2211 / IEP 2211 (sapcpiorderexchange, sapcpiorderexchangeb2b) | https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/9b7fba7fb23645fcb03d294ddee98752/9e522de302d047bdb911b0aa7acd1070.html?version=2211 ; https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_COMMERCE_INTEGRATIONS/9b7fba7fb23645fcb03d294ddee98752/ad0e573097d94005a1e9cf26d7df888f.html?version=2211 | 2026-09-24 | PS-7-114 |
| BUNDLES AND GUIDED SELLING IN THE TARGET: A BACKEND BUNDLE API EXISTS, A COMPOSABLE STOREFRONT FEATURE DOES NOT, AND GUIDED SELLING COMES ONLY WITH SAP CPQ. (1) The 2211 page 'configurablebundleocc Extension' (bin/modules/configurable-bundle/configurablebundleocc) states it 'exposes saved functionality of the Configurable Bundle module in the form of the standardized OCC ... API' with endpoints to create, edit and delete configurable bundles and to retrieve a cart displaying a hierarchy of products. (2) The composable storefront Feature Compatibility Matrix has no bundle or configurable-bundle row (checked by text search of the matrix for 'bundle'); the only composable integration naming bundling is 'SAP CPQ Configurable Products Integration ... includes features like bundling and guided selling' (Integrations page), which needs the separately licensed SAP CPQ product. (3) Commerce bundle templates are maintained in Commerce; the documentation read does not describe an automatic explosion of an S/4HANA sales BOM into Commerce cart sub-items. | S/4HANA ORDER MANAGEMENT ORDER-DETAIL ADD-ONS ARE ODATA-V2-ONLY: Subsequent Document Flow, Order Attachments and Billing Invoice. 'Subsequent Document Flow in Order Display' (IEP 2211) states 'With the 2211-jdk21.3 release, B2B customers can see the document flow and the relevant successor documents for an SAP S/4HANA sales order on the Order Details page' via the Sales Order (A2X) API header/item subsequent process flow, switched on in Backoffice (Display Sales Order Subsequent Documents); 'Currently, this feature is not available with SAP S/4HANA OData V4 APIs'; it 'doesn't retrieve documents stored as links to external systems'. Billing Invoice: 'enable B2B customers to view billing details on the Order Details page', PDF retrieval for billed orders, 'not available with SAP S/4HANA OData V4 APIs'. The Features page lists Quote Creation, Subsequent Document Flow, Order Attachments and Billing Invoice as not supported with OData V4. The IEP Feature List by Release prints Display Subsequent Document Flow as '2211-jdk21.2 (Preview of 2211-jdk21.3)' and Order Attachments as 2211.36 (quoted as printed). Composable Feature Compatibility Matrix: Invoice Display for SAP S/4HANA Order Management Integration 6.5 / 2211.12. | COMPOSABLE STOREFRONT B2B REORDER REPLACES THE ACTIVE CART WITH THE WHOLE PREVIOUS ORDER. The 2211 page 'B2B Reorder' (Storefront Development Guide) states: 'you can use the Reorder button to add all of the items from a previous order to your cart', shown on the Order Details page; Note verbatim: 'When you use the reorder feature, the active cart is replaced by a new cart that contains only the items from the order you are viewing on the Order Details page. Any items that were previously in your cart are effectively removed.' Enabled by installing @spartacus/order; CMS-driven via the AccountOrderDetailsReorderComponent (CMSFlexComponent, slot BodyContent-orderdetail); 'No special extensibility is available for this feature.'
